# Orbweaver — Enforcement Registry: Active Gates

> The currently-live, machine-enforced gate catalog — what fails a build today, across the five active layers (Biome, ts-morph structural gates, dependency-cruiser, jscpd, Stryker; the former GritQL layer is RETIRED — §Layer 2). Not-yet-active + rejected gates: `Core-Enforcement-Deferred-Dropped.md`. Extracted adoption sagas + dropped-experiment postmortems: `history/enforcement-archaeology-record.md`.

---

## Enforcement registry

The single catalog of every machine-enforced invariant in orbweaver, and the deferred backlog of
gates that are deliberately not on yet (with the trigger that turns each one on). If a rule isn't
here, it isn't enforced; if it's in the backlog, it has a named activation condition — nothing is
"forgotten," it's "scheduled."

Philosophy: gates are written **before** the code they govern, so code is born compliant. A gate that
would only false-fire on the current (placeholder) tree is not "missing" — it's in the backlog, keyed
to the code that makes it meaningful.

**The fast lane** — `pnpm check` = `biome` (lint+format) → `eslint` (the doc-comment gates
`tsdoc/syntax` + `@typescript-eslint/no-deprecated` on server/kit/db/contracts, plus the react-surface
rules on ui/client/tests-ui) → `tsc` (types, hardened by `@total-typescript/ts-reset` via the root
`reset.d.ts`) → `test:types` (the vitest typecheck lane over `tests/`) → `check:structure` (ts-morph/fs
gates) → `depcruise` (import graph — ACTIVE, Layer 4). All six must be green. lefthook runs the **full
`pnpm check` at pre-commit** and **`pnpm verify --push` at pre-push** (the static bundle + the node
lanes + the CT suite + `e2e:smoke` — see `lefthook.yml`). There is NO standing CI (the D62 no-CI
ruling): `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is `workflow_dispatch`-only (manual `pnpm verify --full` on
demand). The check budget is **structural-fast** — whole-tree/slow analyses (jscpd, mutation) are
deliberately on-demand lanes, not pre-commit.

**The on-demand lanes** — `pnpm cpd` (jscpd, Layer 5) and `pnpm test:mutation[:gate]` (Stryker,
Layer 6). Enforced (they fail the build), just not in the pre-commit budget.

---

## Layer 1 — Biome (`biome.json`)

~180 explicit rules at `error`, 4 type-aware domains (project/types/react/test), all `warn`→`error`.
Format + lint + import-organize. See `biome.json` and `docs/architecture/core/Spine-TypeScript-and-Patterns.md`.
Notable ratchets: `noUnresolvedImports`, `useConsistentTypeDefinitions: interface` (object shapes are
interfaces — matches structure §7.4), `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity: 15`, `useMaxParams: 4`,
`useExplicitReturnType`, `noMagicNumbers`. `noConsole` is a **total ban by default**
(`allow: []` — server/db/contracts/kit log through the logger/pino, never raw console); relaxed only
for `packages/client/**` (`info/warn/error` ok in the browser until a client logger lands) and turned
off for `scripts/**` + `tests/**` (console is their output channel).

## Layer 2 — GritQL plugins — **RETIRED (2026-07-15, migrated to Layer 3)**

The GritQL layer is GONE: biome's gritql engine had been core-dumping SILENTLY whenever a plugin
touched `packages/db`/`packages/server` shapes (trpc/drizzle call graphs) — a crash reads as "no
diagnostics", so the layer enforced nothing over exactly the packages it claimed to cover. Every
plugin was recreated 1:1 as a ts-morph structural gate in `tooling/src/verify/gates/` (Layer 3 — where the
rule, its diagnostic, and its inline `mustFlag`/`mustPass` proof live in ONE descriptor); `tools/grit/`
is deleted and `biome.json` `plugins` is empty and stays empty. Do NOT add a grit plugin — add a
Layer-3 gate.

## Layer 3 — Structural gates (`tooling/src/verify/`, ts-morph + fs)

Layout: `harness.ts` (Project loader + Violation runner) and `report.ts` (registry) at the root;
each gate is one module in `gates/`. Add a gate by exporting a valid `gate` in `gates/` — a `defineGate`
policy (the final contract, `contract/policy.ts`) or, until the #1584 cutover, a legacy `GateDescriptor` — and the
mixed loader (`tooling/src/verify/lib/loader.ts`, `loadMixedGateCorpus`) discovers and classifies it by exact contract
identity: a branded `defineGate` result is final, a validated descriptor is legacy, an unbranded lookalike refuses the
load; each contract's dispatcher runs its side and `check:structure` reports ONE roster. All are lenient on absent code
(vacuously pass on the placeholder tree, activate as code lands) and **pinned by
`tests/tooling/check-gates.repo.int.test.ts`** — it derives the roster from the mixed `check:structure` run, asserts
every legacy gate fires on a fixture (a broken AST query can't silently pass; anti-drift), and hands every final
policy's bite to `structure:policy-conformance` (its own `mustFlag` rows through the production dispatcher).

| Gate | Enforces |
| - | - |
| `agent-bridge-lock` | issues #894/#995 — the development `globalThis.__orb` surface has exactly ONE install site, and it is in the sanctioned bridge module: a second assignment anywhere in the client source is a finding at that assignment, and two assignments inside the door itself are a CARDINALITY finding the rogue arm cannot see. Both member spellings count (`globalThis.__orb =`, `window["__orb"] =`); a local object whose key is merely spelled `__orb` does not. The capability and evidence-ring registries must keep their `satisfies` lock over the typed handle/ring vocabularies, so a hand-maintained object cannot shed a member without a compiler error — that check compares the ANNOTATION'S TEXT, a declared SPELLING limit with its own passing row, because no shared reader gives a type-annotation an identity today. The readiness home stays lean on three axes — sanctioned static specifiers, no re-export door, no dynamic `import()` — and the boot entry and router reach the bridge only through it, while the bridge shares that module's exact Promise/query. The mounted CT owes both reasoned proof markers, each governing an executable TOP-LEVEL registration: a marker on a `describe`-nested test, or one with no reason, proves nothing. A verdict about a file that is ABSENT anchors on the first present named subject and names the missing path in its message, because the final report sink refuses a finding outside the effective population. |
| `appearance-carrier-contract` | Final ordinary source policy: each manifest row declares a nonempty executable carrier list. Exact occurrence waivers cannot suppress its hard health sibling. Both consume the dispatcher-fed `appearance-carrier` fact; tests/tooling/verify/lib/appearance-carrier-fact.test.ts preserves the legacy corpus and authority separation. |
| `appearance-carrier-health` | Final hard policy for issue #935: schema/manifest/editor-key equality, owner bindings, carrier populations, distinct arms, live consumers, portal obligations, and boot/hydrated/Snap projection parity. Shares the appearance graph with the occurrence policy and preserves the separate runtime planes. |
| `untrusted-regex-safe-exec` | the ONE server execution boundary for user-authored world-info regex keys — the `testRegexKey` property in `entry/compose/chat.ts` — must be `createRegexTest()` whose `node:vm` watchdog terminates pathological evaluation. The factory is the EXPORTED DECLARATION in the server regex kit, resolved through the shared sealed-origin reader, so an alias or a re-export is the same watchdog and a same-named local helper is not. It does NOT ban dynamic `RegExp` construction elsewhere: construction is not execution. Blindness is now a POPULATION RECEIPT of zero rather than a finding — a canonical property that disappears REFUSES the run (D53) |
| `public-route-body-cap` | a public mutating non-tRPC Hono registration under `entry/http/**` that reads `c.req` body data must expose a structural byte-cap proxy before its handler: `bodyLimit`/house `bodyCap`, or bundle import's exact incremental `stageCapped(..., *_MAX_*_BYTES)` arm. GET and zero-body mutations are controls. A `defineGate` ordinary policy (family `public-route-body-cap`, shared reader `lib/http-route-body.ts`'s ancestor-walk route/body-read attribution); the reported position is the route's own method name. The whole-population blindness census moved to the hard `public-route-body-cap-health` sibling below |
| `public-route-body-cap-health` | the HARD whole-population blindness tripwire for `public-route-body-cap` (same family, same shared reader): an `entry/http/**` population yielding zero mutating routes, or zero body-reading routes among them, means the occurrence policy's census is blind. File-anchored on the canonical `entry/http/index.ts` entry — an absence verdict about the whole population has no authored node to bind an ordinary marker to, so this arm has no suppression door |
| `plugin-dump-guard` | every guest-controlled QuickJS value materialized in `infra/plugin-host/membrane.ts` crosses through `tryDumpGuestValue`, whose iterative `handleSafeToDump` depth/node walk PRECEDES the one raw `ctx.dump`. A guard after dump is RED. `ctx.dump` is the METHOD declared by the installed `quickjs-emscripten-core` package read off the receiver's type, and the guard is the membrane's OWN declaration, so a host-side `logger.dump` and an imported same-named guard are both out of subject. Blindness is now a POPULATION RECEIPT of zero — a membrane with no guest-dump site REFUSES the run (D46) |
| `feature-structure` | domain 8-slot template (index/service/context + contract/ + verbs/) |
| `test-layout` | the canonical test-kind registry owns every supported suffix, mirror class and compatible source extension. Module tests under `tests/<pkg>/` prefix-swap to a real `packages/<pkg>/src` `.ts`/`.tsx` file or directory index; suite kinds are cross-cutting and mirror-exempt; `.spec.ts` is e2e-only; support/e2e helpers are exempt. `tests/tooling/<dir>/` mirrors `tooling/src/<dir>/` when that tool dir exists, while flat/root-config tooling tests remain exempt (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.7). CONVERTED to `defineGate` (#1584, #2061): HARD/error, `analysis: "resource"`, `execution: "entire-population"`, family `mirror-index` over the shared `ops/resource-mirror.ts` `loadMirrorIndex` reader — the three mirror policies are this kind's whole consumer set and this conversion is what WIRED it (it shipped frozen with zero declarers). It declares BOTH mirror families (`package-test` for the `tests/` walk and the package source questions, `tooling-test` for the §4.7 arm's DIRECTORY question) and `population: { of: "none" }` — it reads no source file, only membership. The four `existsSync` probes and the recursive `readdirSync` of `tests/` are gone, and with them the blindness they carried: an ABSENT `tests/` tree used to answer every mirror question exactly like a present tree with no member, i.e. as a clean corpus; it is now a population-phase REFUSAL and the run is not a verdict |
| `test-world-browser-contracts` | Spine-Testing.md §1 — Node-intent tests and helpers cannot consume browser-authored UI/client contracts or direct Base UI modules merely because React's Node-world `global.d.ts` supplies empty DOM interfaces. The gate derives its Node/browser scope from the canonical test-kind registry, follows imported/re-exported/namespace/runtime binding identity to the authored module, rejects canonical React DOM-bearing type shapes, and fails loud on unresolved relevant origins. Browser `.dom.*`/CT kinds and DOM-free `.ts` contracts remain legal |
| `tooling-slot-template` | Hard resource policy over the closed tooling authored tree and the @tooling compiler population: enforces the five-slot template, flat \_shared, and live bash/corpus classifications. Snap engine entry imports derive which tools owe no CLI. |
| `tooling-front-door` | cross-tool imports enter via the sibling's index.ts (`#<tool>`); cli.ts consumes only its own index.ts (+ per-module \_shared) — the lane-speed AST arm of the .dependency-cruiser.cjs tooling stanzas (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.2). A final `defineGate` policy (`tooling-front-door` family over `lib/tooling-import-door.ts`, `ordinary`, `selected-files`, `@tooling`): two arms with distinct messages, a cross-tool DEEP import and a cli.ts reaching past its own index.ts; the relative escape out of tooling/ is the reviewed sibling's arm. Waiver position: the quoted module specifier (`@orb-waive tooling-front-door("<specifier>")`). Proofs on the conformance stage; identity arm and the §4.6 differential in `tests/tooling/verify/gates/tooling-front-door-family.test.ts` |
| `tooling-root-config-import` | a relative import out of `tooling/src/` is RED — cross-package needs go through `@orb/*` specifiers — except a one-home ROOT CONFIG read licensed by an exact reviewed grant `(consumer, root-config-import:<config>)`: the one row is `ast/ops/prodonly.ts` × `knip.ts`, whose data would otherwise be re-spelled (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.2). A final `defineGate` policy (`tooling-front-door` family, `reviewed-grant`, `entire-population`): every escape is a finding deduped per `(subject, operation)`; the gate-owned root-config exemption table is GONE and its two-sided stale sweep is now central grant liveness over the reviewed-grant rows in `lib/reviewed-grants.ts`, and a narrowed request DEFERS the policy instead of adjudicating a row its scope cannot see (the 2026-08-30 scoped-run defect, retired by construction) |
| `tooling-size` | tooling/src file caps: 450 default, cli.ts 200; verify/gates/\*\* is the declared carve — what forces the monoliths (snap 4,513 / ast 6,241) to decompose BEFORE moving (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.3) |
| `tooling-project-home` | ONE ts-morph loader — a `new Project(` under `tooling/src/**` outside `_shared/ts-workspace.ts` is a second workspace walk (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.4, arm A of the retired `tooling-shared-plumbing`). A final `defineGate` policy (singleton family, `reviewed-grant`, `analysis: "types"`, `entire-population`): the constructor is judged by IDENTITY against ts-morph's own `Project` export through `lib/project-home-origin.ts#readPackageExportOrigin` — an aliased import is caught, a local class of the name passes, an unplaceable callee is reported fail-closed — and the loader home plus the eight censused NON-workspace constructions (what the gate's own deleted home/project-site tables used to hold, now reviewed-grant rows in `lib/reviewed-grants.ts`, plus `verify/ops/policy-conformance.ts`, whose three Projects the legacy census never listed and which was RED under the legacy gate on the live tree) are nine exact `(file, ts-morph-project-construction)` grants in `lib/reviewed-grants.ts`; the legacy two-sided stale sweep is central grant liveness |
| `tooling-browser-door` | ONE Playwright launch and ONE attach — `<engine>.launch(` outside `launchProbeSession` and `<engine>.connect(`/`.connectOverCDP(` outside `attachProbeSession` (both `_shared/browser.ts`) are a second bootstrap and a second ProbeSession shape (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.4, arms B + H; docs/design/1208-instrument-substrate.md §3.4). A final `defineGate` policy (singleton, `reviewed-grant`, `analysis: "types"`, `entire-population`): the receiver is judged as Playwright's own engine export over the three doors that ship it (`@playwright/test`/`playwright`/`playwright-core`), so an aliased engine is caught and a local object named `chromium` passes; puppeteer's `connect` stays the declared limit; the home carries two exact grants, `browser-launch` and `browser-attach` |
| `tooling-artifact-path-home` | ONE spelling of the `reports/…` artifact root — a `"reports"`/`"reports/…"` literal (or a const holding it) fed to a `join`/`resolve`/`mkdir`/`mkdirSync` that resolves to node's path/fs doors, outside `_shared/artifacts.ts` (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.4, arm C). A final `defineGate` policy (family `tooling-artifact` over `lib/artifact-filing.ts`, `reviewed-grant`, `analysis: "types"`, `entire-population`): the callee is judged by IDENTITY, so `path.join(REPO_ROOT, "reports", …)` — live at `model-ab/ops/run.ts:110` for the tool's whole life and invisible to the legacy identifier-only match — is caught (and was fixed at conversion); the home carries the one exact grant `reports-path-literal` |
| `tooling-artifact-run-slot` | a tool that FILES an artifact (`artifactDir`/`artifactFile`, `_shared/artifact-out.ts`) opens a run slot (`withInstrumentRun`) in its `cli.ts` — the #1164 clobber class (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.4, arm G; UNIFIED-VERIFICATION-DESIGN.md §3.3b). A final `defineGate` policy (family `tooling-artifact`, `hard`, `analysis: "types"`, `entire-population`): both doors are judged by identity against the LOCATED home (receipted, one receipt per half — an absent home refuses the run), fail-closed both ways — an unplaceable filer still files, an unplaceable opener opens nothing — and the finding anchors on the tool's first filing call, naming the cli.ts that owes the slot |
| `tooling-process-exit-home` | ONE hard exit — `process.exit(` outside `_shared/run-tool.ts` drops unflushed stdout and dodges the exit-honesty runner (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.4, arm D). A final `defineGate` policy (family `process-member` over `lib/process-member-origin.ts`, with the argv pair; `reviewed-grant`, `analysis: "types"`, `entire-population`): the receiver is judged by IDENTITY as the real `process` (the ambient global or the `node:process` default export), both the dotted and the `process["exit"]` spelling; a local object named `process` passes and an unplaceable receiver is reported fail-closed; the runner carries the one exact grant `process-exit` |
| `tooling-cli-entry` | every tool `cli.ts` enters through `runTool` (`_shared/run-tool.ts`) — a hand-rolled entry exits with whatever node decided (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.4, arm E). A final `defineGate` policy (family `tooling-program-entry` with `tooling-ops-direct-invocation`, `hard`, `analysis: "types"`, `entire-population` over `tooling/src/*/cli.ts` plus the runner home): the callee must resolve BY DECLARATION to the runner home's `runTool` export (a local function of the name is no entry, an aliased import is, an unplaceable callee is reported); the home is located and receipted, so an absent runner refuses the run. Declared limit carried from the legacy: the call may sit anywhere in the cli, not only at module scope |
| `tooling-child-process-door` | ONE subprocess home — a `node:child_process`/`child_process` import outside `_shared/proc.ts` bypasses the nice -19 floor, and a call to a full-priority door (`spawnFullPrioritySync`/`spawnFullPriorityChild`) is licensed per caller, never ambient (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.4, arms F + F2). A final `defineGate` policy (singleton, `reviewed-grant`, `analysis: "types"`, `entire-population`): the import is matched by specifier, the doors by DECLARATION IDENTITY against the located, receipted home (a local function of a door's name is not the door — the un-niced spawn it would need is the import arm's finding); five exact grants in `lib/reviewed-grants.ts`: `(proc.ts, child-process-import)` and four `(caller, full-priority-spawn)` — what the gate's own deleted home row and full-priority caller census used to hold |
| `tooling-port-registry` | every TCP port is a row in `_shared/ports.ts` — a numeric literal whose VALUE is a registry port (reserved or stage-band; the registry is imported, so a new row widens the arm for free) or one at a PORT-NAMED position carrying an undeclared number, anywhere in `tooling/src/**` or `tests/e2e/support/**` (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.4, the tree half of arm I; docs/design/1208-instrument-substrate.md §3.6). A final `defineGate` policy (family `plumbing-literals` over `lib/plumbing-literals.ts#portLiteralOf`, `reviewed-grant`, `analysis: "syntax"`, `entire-population`); the registry carries the one exact grant `port-literal`. Declared limits carried verbatim: a port inside a STRING is not judged, and the four MIRROR sites (`stack.sh`/`multi-user-fixture.sh`/`engines.sh`; `packages/client/vite.config.ts`) are excluded BY OWNER RULING 2026-09-02, named in ports.ts's header |
| `tooling-clock-budget` | every WALL CLOCK is `budget(<X>_BASE_MS)` (`_shared/load-budget.ts`) — a numeric literal at a `timeout`/`timeoutMs`/`testTimeout`/`hookTimeout`/`actionTimeout`/`navigationTimeout` option, a `*_TIMEOUT_MS` const with a bare numeric initializer, or a ceiling-scale `setTimeout`, anywhere in `tooling/src/**` or `tests/tooling/**` (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.4, the tree half of arm J; docs/design/1208-instrument-substrate.md §7.1, #1232). A final `defineGate` policy (family `plumbing-literals` over `lib/plumbing-literals.ts#fixedClockOf`, the split's ONE `ordinary` policy, `analysis: "syntax"`, `selected-files`): the reported position is the NUMERIC LITERAL, so a deliberately fixed clock (fixture data the run does not pay) takes `@orb-waive tooling-clock-budget(<literal>)` at its site — the gate's own deleted shrink-only clock-site census was two per-site arguments, which is the waiver shape, so it became `@orb-waive` markers rather than reviewed-grant rows, and its one live row is translated; the eight fixed clocks that drifted in after the census closed (RED under the legacy gate on the live tree) were routed through `budget`/`scaledBudget` at conversion |
| `tooling-runner-config-literals` | the three ROOT runner configs (`vitest.config.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`, `playwright-ct.config.ts`) spell every wall clock as `budget(<base>)` and every port as a `_shared/ports.ts` row (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.4, the root-config halves of arms I and J). A final `defineGate` policy (family `plumbing-literals` over `lib/plumbing-literals.ts#runnerConfigLiteralFacts`, `hard`, `analysis: "resource"`, `entire-population`, population `{ of: "none" }`): the configs are three `exact-file` resources (`vitest-config`/`playwright-config`/`playwright-ct-config`, minted in `contract/resource-exact.ts` with this policy as their named consumer) walked through the one scratch parser; a missing config is the door's own fail-closed refusal, an unparseable one a finding. Declared limit: the closed ids name the three files that exist — a fourth runner config is unjudged until it gets an id (the legacy discovered `playwright*.config.ts` by readdir). New evidence at conversion: `playwright.config.ts` carried three literal clocks the legacy never read; they go through `budget` now with their quiet-box values preserved |
| `tooling-argv-front-door` | issue #971 — the OPERATOR'S ARGV enters a tooling program at exactly ONE place and flows DOWN as a `readonly string[]` parameter: a `process.argv` read (dotted OR the `process["argv"]` element-access spelling) is legal only in a tool's `cli.ts` — derived by SHAPE, so a cli.ts that moves reds at its new path — or in a REVIEWED entry: the node half a `.sh` execs, which has no cli.ts by §2.5, plus `_shared/entrypoint.ts`, whose subject is `argv[1]`, the ENTRY IDENTITY, not the operator's flags. A library reading the GLOBAL argv makes its behaviour depend on how the process was started (the census at mint found four such readers). A final `defineGate` policy (family `process-member` over `lib/process-member-origin.ts`, shared with `tooling-process-exit-home`; `reviewed-grant`, `analysis: "types"`, `entire-population`): the receiver is judged by IDENTITY — the ambient `process` or the `node:process` default export, never its text — so a local object named `process` passes and an unreadable receiver is reported fail-closed; every non-cli read is one `(file, process-argv-read)` finding, the six rows of the gate's own deleted argv-entry table are six exact grants in `lib/reviewed-grants.ts`, and its two-sided stale sweep is now central grant liveness. Two policies rather than the three first ruled: an ordinary twin over the same predicate would double-report every site, and the derivable partition (a module-scope `runTool` program as a front door) was refused because it narrows the catch. DECLARED LIMIT, as a mustPass row: the policy pins WHERE argv is read, never how strictly each tool's own grammar parses it (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.9) |
| `tooling-argv-front-door-health` | the §4.6 BLINDNESS TRIPWIRE for `tooling-argv-front-door`, its own HARD `entire-population` policy (same family and reader): zero tool `cli.ts` readers of `process.argv` on a run where the real-tree anchor `tooling/src/_shared/exit-contract.ts` is loaded means the matcher stopped recognising the read and every reviewed arm is vacuously green — ONE file-level finding at the anchor. Self-guards off when the anchor is absent, and a narrowed request DEFERS it |
| `tooling-ops-direct-invocation` | every `tooling/src/<tool>/ops/**` module either calls `refuseDirectInvocation` at MODULE SCOPE or IS a program (a module-scope call to the one entry runner, `runTool` — stack.sh's node halves). Closes #509's lying-instrument class at the source: an ops module is a LIBRARY, so `node <that path>` loads it, executes nothing and exits 0 — a bare zero every reader takes for a clean verdict. A final `defineGate` policy (family `tooling-program-entry` with `tooling-cli-entry`, `hard`, `analysis: "types"`, `entire-population`): the module-scope calls are read statement-level through `_shared/ts-workspace.ts#moduleScopeCalls` (AST-POSITIONAL, never a text search — a guard spelled inside a template string or a comment is no guard) and each callee is judged by DECLARATION IDENTITY against the two located `_shared` homes through `lib/project-home-origin.ts` — a local function of the guard's name is no guard, an aliased import is, and a callee the readers cannot place is reported. NO path allowlist — the entry case is DERIVED (a module that ends in `runTool` cannot lie about doing nothing), so there is no row to rot. The two homes are located and RECEIPTED one receipt each, so an absent home or a renamed export REFUSES the run at the receipt phase (the legacy blindness finding, now the runtime's own tool error — pinned in `tests/tooling/verify/gates/tooling-ops-direct-invocation.test.ts` with the §4.6 differential). Behavioral twin: `tests/tooling/_shared/entrypoint.int.test.ts` RUNS every ops module and asserts exit 2 |
| `tooling-instrument-proof` | every INSTRUMENT_TOOLS member owes TWO reasoned proof classes in its tests/tooling mirror — `@instrument-proof:` (a planted DEFECT must RED) and `@instrument-absence-proof:` (a removed apparatus / emptied population must NOT read clean, the #409 class) — one finding per missing class; both vocabularies two-sided; registered tools must route verdict output through `_shared/evidence.ts#printVerdict` and may not bypass it with a direct `_shared/artifacts.ts#printResult` call (arm F); plus the dead-registry-row and registry-blindness tripwires (Core-Tooling-Law\.md §4.5). INSTRUMENT_TOOLS is RESOLVED through `lib/tuple-read.ts` (#947), so a member spread in from a sibling module (`[...CORE_INSTRUMENTS, "snap"]`) still owes both proof classes and reports at its OWN declaring line, and an unsanctioned composition shape refuses loudly. A member's home is judged by the DECLARED authored-path identity door rather than by bare existence, which gives three verdicts instead of two: a live DIRECTORY passes, a missing path or a same-named FILE is a dead registry row, and a selector that resolves outside the checkout — including through an in-repo symlink, which existence and tracked membership both wave through — is UNJUDGED and says so. Arm F judges the verdict door by CANONICAL EXPORT IDENTITY against the located artifacts home, so an alias, either namespace member spelling and a name-preserving re-export are all the same door, a same-named export of another module is not, and a home that cannot be located at all is arm F's own blindness verdict rather than a pass. A marker in a FLAT `tests/tooling` file mirrors no tool and counts for nothing |
| `design-audit-rule-proof` | issue #882 — ONE `as const` registry is the live design-audit denominator, and every id in it owes BOTH an executable firing proof and an executable nearest-legitimate-neighbour silence proof. **The evidence is an executable `auditRuleTest(rows, title, fn)` REGISTRATION, not a comment marker** — the `@rule-fires` / `@rule-silent` comment vocabulary this row used to name was retired when the gate moved to rows, and a stale fixture kept the gate silently UNFIRED for weeks afterwards. A registration counts only when its helper is bound to the file's ONE unaliased imported `test`, is not shadowed at file or helper scope, forwards the helper's own `title`/`fn`, sits at TOP LEVEL, and carries a literal row array and an inline callback; rows resolve through immutable bindings, so a const-bound array is still a literal one. Missing pairs, stale ids, malformed rows, an unreadable kind outside `fires`/`silent`, an empty reason, duplicate registry ids, a registry id that is not a readable authored string, a zero-population registry and an absent registry are each RED with their own verdict. Findings anchor on the AUTHORED NODE they are about — the registry row's `id`, the proof row's `rule` — except the absence verdict, which anchors on the first present named subject because the report sink refuses a finding outside the effective population |
| `devtools-frontend-assets` | issue #950 — Snap's declaration-winner receipt runs only against the committed revision-matched official DevTools frontend SDK closure. A final `defineGate` policy (singleton family, `hard`, `analysis: "resource"`, `execution: "entire-population"`, `population: { of: "none" }`) declaring THREE closed resources — `devtools-closure` for the vendored root, plus `installed-package` `playwright-test`/`metadata` and `playwright-core`/`text browsers.json` for the browser tuple the pin is held against — and handing them to `_shared/devtools-assets.ts#adjudicateDevToolsClosure`, the PURE adjudicator the loopback runtime's own `verifyDevToolsAssetsSync` delegates to, so both callers reach one verdict through one algorithm. It judges the exact Playwright/Chrome/Chromium/DevTools/protocol tuple, manifest checksum, every resource hash/size/MIME/path, complete revision-pinned license notices, exact inventory, and the canonical-relative/no-symlink boundary; missing, extra, zero-population, mismatched, or tuple-drifted bytes are RED before any loopback server starts. ONE finding per broken closure (the adjudicator throws on its first violation), anchored at the pin with the synthetic `asset-contract` token, so there is no ordinary position and no waiver door. An ABSENT or unreadable closure is no longer a finding: the declaration is non-ready, the owner is WITHHELD at the population phase and the run is a tool error — the runtime accusing rather than the policy guessing. The closure's exact resource count and decoded size are the committed pin (`tooling/src/snap/lib/devtools-frontend/pin.json` `resourceCount`/`decodedBytes`) and the gate's own scan line, never a number restated here; the closure includes the official modules' conditional literal asset edges, not only the responses a scratch render happened to request. Runtime serves verified in-memory bytes by exact manifest URL over IPv4 loopback GET/HEAD only; the networked materializer is a maintainer command, never a runtime fallback |
| `verb-naming` | `verbs/<v>.ts` exports a callable runtime `create<Pascal(v)>` value; the unwrapped runtime initializer/declaration itself must be callable, so type-only exports, non-callable values, and callable annotations/assertions over impossible initializers do not satisfy the gate |
| `no-caller-user-id` | the identifier `callerUserId` is forbidden (D19 turn-identity): the caller is `Principal.userId`; use `triggeredBy`/`runAsUserId`. AST-only (catches a newly-introduced name tsc can't) |
| `types-in-contract` | a feature's `contract/service.ts` declares the exported `<Feature>Service` interface (§7.4) |
| `no-inline-union-redecl` | a string-union AXIS is declared ONCE as an `as const` tuple and the union DERIVED, never re-spelled (Spine-TypeScript-and-Patterns.md §7.5). A final `defineGate` policy — singleton family over the shared `union-axis` reader, `ordinary`, `analysis: "types"`, `entire-population` — with two arms and one authority. ARM A: an inline string-literal union TYPE ALIAS of ≥3 members, reported at the ALIAS NAME. ARM B: any other inline string-literal SET — a union in a property/return/argument position, or a `z.enum([...])` array — whose members EXACTLY equal a canonical tuple collected from the WHOLE tree, reported at the SET's own source text (the z.enum sub-kind anchors on the ARRAY argument, because a call's parens cannot be a marker position), with the matched tuple's name and home in the message. Exact-set identity, never a heuristic: a genuine one-off enum with no canonical tuple (the NODE_ENV class) is not flagged. A tuple registers as canonical at ≥3 members, or ≥2 when homed in `packages/contracts/`/`packages/kit/` where a cross-boundary axis lives by law; a `satisfies readonly Interface["prop"][]` tuple registers through the SatisfiesExpression wrapper and its own indexed-access co-declaration is EXEMPT, since that union is the source the tuple derives from rather than a re-spell of it. Arm B never flags UP the package cake — a re-spell whose matching home its package cannot import (kit against contracts; the sealed `ui` against its rank-peer contracts, D54) would demand an illegal import. `entire-population` is load-bearing: arm B matches across packages, so a narrowed request DEFERS rather than concluding an axis is unhomed. Gate modules are subtracted from the population because proof fixtures re-spell axes by design. Declared limits, each with a `mustPass` row: a numeric tuple or a union carrying a non-string member is not a string axis; an ordinary call taking the same literal array is passing data, not declaring one. An unresolvable `satisfies` clause loses its exemption and fails OPEN, and two re-spells of one set inside one statement share a carrier and a position |
| `test-presence` | verbs/persistence/contract-schemas carry their required `.test`/`.int.test`/`.contract.test`, and the DOMAIN arm is demand-by-default (#767): any other `domain/**` file with runtime logic — `substrate/`, a named subsystem, `guard.ts`, a `contract/` file carrying real logic — owes a mirror `.test`/`.int.test` unless its SHAPE exempts it (`index.ts`, the zero-logic `service.ts`/`context.ts` roots, an error-declaration-only `contract/` file, a D58 stub runner). The `entry/` + `transport/` TIER arm is the same shape (#773): a boot step, composition seam, HTTP registrar, job driver, bus or ladder primitive with runtime logic owes a mirror `.test`/`.int.test` unless it is a `.d.ts`, a router shell (no callable export), or a PASS-THROUGH wiring file — every exported callable being ONE delegating call or ONE DI-bundle object literal over its own parameters, which is what `core/Tier-5-Entry.md` invariant 1 and `core/Tier-4-Transport.md` say those tiers are allowed to contain. THE SHRINK-ONLY DEBT RATCHET IS RETIRED (#2062): `test-presence.baseline.json`, its `writeBaseline` single writer, the `baseline test-presence` verb and the `pnpm debt` row are deleted. Its two rows — `domain/chat/substrate/{assembly-access,turn-access}.ts` — were a demand this policy should never have made: both are pure SPREAD-FORWARDING DI bridges existing only to mediate `domain-no-cross-subsystem`, and the DOMAIN residual arm lacked the PASS-THROUGH exemption the TIER arm has had since #773. **A DELIBERATE WIDENING of this gate's exemption, landed 2026-09-12**, closing an asymmetry rather than minting an exemption: `isPlumbingAtom` now accepts a spread of a plumbing atom and `pushDomainResidual` applies `isPassThroughWiring`. METHOD — both predicates re-implemented verbatim in a throwaway probe over `packages/server/src/**` and run WITH and WITHOUT each clause, diffing the admitted sets: the spread clause changes exactly those two files and ZERO in entry/transport; the domain clause matches 11 files, 9 of which already carry mirror tests. The exemption is BY SHAPE, so a seam that grows a branch is demanded again. The cited burn-down item #772 was CLOSED while both subjects were still live debt, and guide §12.5 permits a retiring baseline only a fix, an exact grant, or warning debt on a POSITIVE LIVE issue — the warning arm being inexpressible because `severity` is per-POLICY. CONVERTED to `defineGate` (#1584, #2061): HARD/warning under temporary owner #2346, `analysis: "resource"`, `execution: "entire-population"`, family `mirror-index` over the shared `ops/resource-mirror.ts` `loadMirrorIndex` reader — the three mirror policies are this kind's whole consumer set and this conversion is what WIRED it (it shipped frozen with zero declarers). Its one declaration is `mirror-index:package-test`; the `existsSync` mirror probe and the `getSourceFiles` walk are gone. Missing-test findings remain effective and unwaived, contribute zero to default blocking, and block under `--fail-on-warnings`; the two zero-recognition blindness diagnoses refuse evaluation, produce no partial findings, and withhold the owner. Closeout clears the derived raw population, restores HARD/error, and removes #2346 without adding a marker or grant door |

| `commented-code` | no parked code in `//` comments (prose only) |
| `schema-branding` | ORDINARY/error, `analysis: types`, `execution: entire-population`, `facts: [drizzleSchemaFact]`. Flags a primary-key column named `id` with no canonical `.$type<XId>()` from `@orb/kit/ids`, and an FK column whose brand differs from its BRANDED parent — a parent that carries no brand at all is out of scope (a deliberately plain identity, not a mismatch). Reads the shared `lib/schema-fact.ts` provider's `drizzleSchemaFact`, never walks source directly. **The waiver position is the column's property name** (`@orb-waive schema-branding(<column>): <reason>`) — a deliberate plain identity uses this on the declaration line. |
| `db-structure` | HARD/error schema barrel integrity over the database source program. Every top-level schema module is re-exported from the resolved aggregator; a missing barrel is a finding. Producer ownership is judged separately by `db-structure-producer-home`. |
| `db-structure-producer-home` | Reviewed-grant/error resource policy comparing schema modules with server producer domains through declared authored trees. Reserved structural modules are classified separately; deliberate non-domain producers require exact central grants. Missing or unreadable resources refuse rather than imply an absent producer. |
| `fk-columns-indexed` | every `.references()` column LEADS a B-tree index (`index`/`uniqueIndex`/composite `primaryKey`/an inline `.primaryKey()`/`.unique()`). SQLite auto-indexes only the PARENT side of an FK, so an unindexed child column means every parent delete (CASCADE / SET NULL / the RESTRICT probe) full-scans the child table, and so does every lookup by that column. LEADING, not "mentioned": SQLite only uses an index whose LEFTMOST column is the one constrained, so a column sitting second in a composite is unindexed for exactly those predicates — 8 of the 37 violations it was minted from were that shape (`chat_participants.userId`, covered only by UNIQUE(chatId,userId), left D18's membership read table-scanning). All 37 were FIXED in the minting commit; NO allowlist exists, deliberately (a future genuinely pure-cost case lands as a cited row WITH its own mustPass, never a silent pass). Plain B-tree only — no ANN/vector shadow index (§Esoteric #1). DECLARED limit (NARROWED #945, closed further #1035): the columns argument is resolved through local/imported object-literal bindings and object spreads by the shared Drizzle schema fact's own object-member reader (`drizzleSchemaFact` → its `objectEntries` walk) — NOT by the legacy `_shared/schema-read.ts`, which this policy does not import — so `sqliteTable("x", importedColumns, …)` keeps every FK obligation — and every MEMBER KIND is answered, not just inline properties: a SHORTHAND member resolves through its binding (measured: an FK written `{ id, chatId }` made this gate GREEN where the byte-identical inline column RED'd) and a COMPUTED key resolves when it is a string literal. A columns object built by a CALL, an unresolvable shorthand, a non-literal computed key, a binding that lands on an empty object, and any other member kind are loud refusals, never an FK-less read |
| `fk-ondelete-stated` | every `.references(...)` states an explicit `onDelete`. SQLite's silent default is `NO ACTION` (the parent delete fails at commit) — a real policy, never a defaulted one, and every deletion policy in this schema is load-bearing (D18 membership cascade, D26's attribution SET NULLs, D27's fork self-FK, the RESTRICT probes). PURE PREVENTION: minted at zero violations (all 126 referencing columns already stated it); a deliberate NO ACTION is spelled `{ onDelete: "no action" }` and passes. Fails CLOSED on a spread options object — the action is named AT the column |
| `table-explicit-primary-key` | every `sqliteTable` declares a primary key — an inline `.primaryKey()` (the TypeID-PK norm) or a composite `primaryKey({ columns })` (the junction norm). Without one SQLite falls back to the hidden `rowid`: unstable across VACUUM, invisible to `$inferSelect`, unreferenceable — so the table can never be an FK parent, which in this schema is the same as never being part of the inherited-ownership chain (AGENTS §1). PURE PREVENTION: minted at zero violations, proving all 76 tables every run |
| `baseline-single-migration` | Final hard resource policy: pre-launch migrations contain the exact baseline SQL and one matching journal entry. Extra SQL or journal drift fails; missing baseline acquisition refuses. ResourceHost owns migration-tree, exact-file and strict JSON reads. Launch requires retiring this policy alongside the migration posture change. |
| `sole-env-reader` | `foundation/env` is the ONLY `process.env` reader — AST gate catching the `process["env"]` bracket form biome's `noProcessEnv` misses (ignores comments) CONVERTED (#1584) to a `reviewed-grant` policy: the receiver is resolved by IDENTITY (the ambient global OR the `node:process` default export, which is how every live reader spells it) rather than by the text `process`, and the operation is derived from the read's own shape — a static key carries it, a dynamic key or a whole-bag read does not. Its exceptions are nine exact `(subject, operation)` rows in the central reviewed-grant table (`tooling/src/verify/lib/reviewed-grants.ts`): the `foundation/env` home's four reads plus the sanctioned call-time reader's five governance KEYS, so the legacy per-key ratchet survives as central zero-consumption staleness. The `(subject, operation)` grain is held TWO-SIDED per policy: a row naming the right subject with a drifted operation, and a row naming a renamed subject, each license NOTHING and raise the STALE alarm while the finding stays effective. The RECEIVER identity is held on both branches — the module door by a project module whose default export carries an `env` bag, the ambient name by a second trusted global declaring the same bag under a different name — and the module-branch member-path fence by a nested `env` key. Its ambient-branch twin is a written UNFALSIFIABLE limit: the construction needs an `env` nested inside `NodeJS.Process`, a shape the package does not ship. |
| `assumes-single-replica` | a module-scope mutable per-process accumulator carries `ASSUMES(single-replica)` in its file — a `new Map/Set/WeakMap/WeakSet()` (non-literal-seed), an array mutated via push/shift/splice/unshift anywhere in the module, or an instance of a locally-declared class with a non-readonly field (widened 2026-08-24, #645; DECLARED LIMIT: a module-scope binding to an imported factory call's return, e.g. `createBoundedRing()`, stays out of structural reach). A `defineGate` ordinary policy (singleton family); the reported position is always the declaration's own bare name |
| `byte-check-cast` | a schema `check(...)` CHECK-DDL `length(<col>) <= N` capped by a BYTE-named constant (`*_BYTE`/`*_BYTES`) must cast to blob first (`length(cast(<col> as blob)) <= N`) — SQLite `length()` on TEXT counts code points, so an uncast byte cap silently admits up to 4x its stated limit (#642). `check` and `sql`/`sql.raw` are read through the shared module-origin reader, so an import alias is the same builder while a resolving same-named project export and a local helper are not; the SQL is read through the shared template reader (quasis and holes apart, so a member-valued cap is still named); and the capped COLUMN resolves through the drizzle SCHEMA FACT — which is what acquits a byte cap over a BLOB column, where `length()` already counts bytes, including when the SQL quotes the identifier. A character-intent cap stays silent. DECLARED LIMITS, each with its row: byte intent is NAME-only so an unnameable cap fails QUIET, `json_array_length()` is a different function and out of subject, and the population is the schema directory because outside it there is no table fact to judge the column against |
| `providers-runner-seal` | no module under `domain/`/`transport/`/`entry/` may NAME a sealed runner symbol (`deriveRunner`/`backendForSource`/`BackendKey`/`BACKEND_KEYS`) — `Tier-3b-Providers.md` inv #3. Identity is the sealed DECLARATION HOME (`infra/providers/**`) through the shared sealed-origin reader, so an import alias, a namespace member and a name-preserving re-export are the same sealed symbol and a same-named export of another module is not; a candidate that provably binds a non-module declaration (a local function, a local object's KEY) is NOT A SUBJECT, while an unreadable import DOOR fails CLOSED. DECLARED LIMIT: a barrel re-exporting the symbol under a DIFFERENT name is outside the candidate-name prefilter |

| `ui-exports-map-complete` | `@orb/ui` is EXPORTS-MAP sealed, so a module dir the map does not name is UNIMPORTABLE and nothing else on the tree says so — `hint-trigger` shipped that way for a day and only its CT's failed import found it. A1 a module dir with an `index.ts` and no (or a wrong-target) `"./<name>"` entry; A2 a family subdir with no `index.ts` at all (no front door to export — `ui-primitive-permissions`' trio clause covers only `primitives/`, so `charts`/`content`/`art` had nothing); A3 an entry whose target is gone, or whose target is not a package-relative `./` specifier — two causes, two messages (two-sided: a dead entry re-attaches to whatever later takes that path; a `../` or bare specifier the legacy `existsSync` would have followed is refused as nothing this package owns). THERE IS NO BLINDNESS ARM, deliberately, and the legacy A4 split into its two halves: a manifest with NO `exports` key resolves to an empty map and is a VERDICT (one A1 finding per derived module); a missing or malformed manifest, or an `exports` block that is not a string map, makes `resolveResourceDeclarations` throw in the POPULATION phase and the receipt phase withholds the policy before `create`/`evaluate` run — a TOOL ERROR, never a finding, pinned through `runPolicyPass` in the wave-1 family test because no proof row can express a refusal. An in-module not-ready branch would be unreachable code teaching the next resource conversion to answer a broken resource with a silent return. The tree is declared as the whole `packages` authored tree rather than the narrower ui source tree because the manifest exports a file OUTSIDE `src` (`./token-contract`) that the A3 arm must be able to see. THE SHAPE IS DERIVED, never listed: a depth-1 dir under `src/` WITH its own `index.ts` is a MODULE owning `./<dir>`, one WITHOUT is a FAMILY whose subdirs are the modules — so a new family needs no gate edit and no family list can go stale. DECLARED LIMITS (mustPass rows): a leaf dir that is neither (`styles/`, published by FILE) owns no entry, and a module's own subdirs (`tokens/generated`) are internals |

| `no-parallel-section-map` | client-architecture-lockdown §5 rule 4 / §16 G2 — the composition bug was one section (or modal, config group, chrome widget) smeared across parallel static maps no gate forced to agree. FIVE arms outside the sanctioned homes: an object literal whose NAMED keys are all vocabulary ids (≥2 — the deleted `SECTION_PANEL_DEFAULTS`/`YOU_MODAL_ROWS`); an array of `{ id: … }` elements covering ≥2 ids (`RAIL_SECTIONS`/`RAIL_ACTIONS`); a bare array of ≥2 distinct id STRING literals, `x as const` wrappers included (the deleted you-modal id-array shape — zero object elements, so the `{id:…}` arm cannot see it); a `Record<VocabId, …>`/`Partial<Record<VocabId, …>>` annotation on a MODULE-LEVEL value declaration (the map a call built, which no literal carries); and the CHROME arm, which keys on the `CHROME_ZONES` VALUE axis because chrome has no id tuple (shell-chrome-unification §D/§E-7). Every arm demands PURE vocabulary space — one foreign key, `id`, string or non-chrome `zone` and it is an incidental collision, not a parallel map — and a DERIVED map (`registry.list().filter…`) carries neither literal keys nor the annotation, so deriving from the registry passes. Homes are per-vocabulary: the tuple's own state module, the `main.tsx`/`compose/` door, and the vocabulary's co-located definition files, judged through the family's shared `isDefinitionHome` slot reader rather than a private regex; chrome adds `state/assemble-chrome.ts`. EVERY VOCABULARY IS RESOLVED, NEVER READ FLAT (#942): `readTupleDeclaration` follows a sanctioned spread of a local or imported sibling tuple and refuses loudly on any other composition shape, so the live `CHROME_ZONES = [...RAIL_ZONES, "topbar.trail"]` is FOUR zones — a direct-element reader saw ONE and every `rail.*`-zoned parallel map escaped while the file scan stayed healthy. CONVERTED to `defineGate` (#1584, 2026-09-12): `ordinary`/`error`, population `@client` (byte-identical port of the legacy `path.includes("/packages/client/src/")` filter, 8d93d820f), family `registry-definitions`; `lib/tuple-read.ts` is a shared PRIMITIVE it reads, not a second family. THE BLINDNESS DOOR REPLACED A SILENT SKIP: legacy's `if (vocab.ids.size === 0) continue` retired an arm without saying so, and the conversion files ONE POPULATION RECEIPT PER VOCABULARY, so a name no exported declaration claims, one TWO declarations claim, and an empty tuple each refuse the run and WITHHOLD the policy (pinned in `tests/tooling/verify/gates/registry-family.test.ts`). THE `Record<…>` ARM IS MODULE-LEVEL ONLY, and that fence is measured rather than inherited: the visitor sees function-scoped declarations the legacy top-level reader could not, and the one new real-tree finding that produced was a FALSE POSITIVE — a render-time `Partial<Record<SectionId, ReactNode>>` accumulator filled by a loop over `registry.list()`, which is the derivation this law asks for. ESCAPE: ONE adjacent `// @orb-waive no-parallel-section-map(<position>): <why + end condition>` — the position is the DISCRIMINATING MEMBER, which is the first vocabulary key for an object map, the first covered `id`/zone string literal WITH its quotes for an array, and the DECLARED NAME for a `Record<…>`-annotated declaration |

CONVERTED (#1584) to an `ordinary` `types` policy; each arm anchors on the authored token a waiver binds to (`useResolved`, `tabs`, the type argument's own first token or the callee, `bodies`, `claims`, the bare class name) where the legacy arms carried NO token and had no working waiver door. **Arms 6 and 8 SPLIT OUT** into `context-definition-shape-health` |

| `zustand-selector-derived` | the DEEP half of the Zustand selector-stability belt (UI-Lib-Zustand.md §A/§C-1, UI-Gates-and-Lessons.md §7/§11.5) — v5 dropped v4's implicit shallow compare, so a selector that mints a fresh reference every render never satisfies `Object.is` and spins `useSyncExternalStore` forever. It judges the selector's whole OUTPUT SET (the concise body, every `return` belonging directly to a BLOCK body, and either branch of a `?:`/`??`/`\|\|`/`&&`) against three derivation shapes: a fresh object/array literal, an `Object.keys/values/entries(...)` derivation, and an array-rebuilding `.map/.filter/...`. CONVERTED (#1584) to an `ordinary` syntax policy over `@client`; **the waiver position is the STORE HOOK NAME the call is made on** (`@orb-waive zustand-selector-derived(useXStore): <reason>`) and the derivation kind rides the MESSAGE, because the legacy token `fresh Object.keys(...) derivation` contains parens the marker grammar's position group can never parse and is not authored text besides. ITS SUBJECT IS THE CALLEE'S TEXT — `/^use[A-Z].*Store$/` plus the vanilla `useStore(store, selector)` adapter — which is why it is a declared SINGLETON family and not a sibling of `zustand-selector-stability`, whose subject is the callee's resolved TYPE identity: neither reader subsumes the other (the type reader catches a store hook that breaks the naming convention; this one catches a body shape the type reader never inspects and reaches the vanilla adapter, whose `useStore` is not a bound-hook alias), so the pre-existing double report on a concise literal is deliberate and unchanged. A MERGE of the two under one stronger reader is open on #1584. The legacy `useShallow` early return was DEAD CODE and is retired — `useShallow(sel)` is a call, which the inline-function test already rejects; the escape still passes and two `mustPass` rows state that mechanism. DECLARED LIMIT (a mustPass row): an indirect by-reference selector is out of a syntax-plane reader's reach |

Two arms over the PD registry read as ONE ledger IDENTITY (both halves — the active table and the cleared ledger — or the fact refuses, because reading half a registry is how a LIVE id reads as an orphan): (a) a `PD-n` id with a second `^\|`-anchored row anywhere across the identity is a concurrent-append collision, reported at the SECOND occurrence with the bare id as its token, not at the registry's line 1 as the legacy descriptor did; (b) a `FLAG[PD-n]` citation in the authored corpus with no row is an orphan, reported at the id INSIDE the bracketed form. A registry row with NO citation is legitimate — the reconciliation is one-sided by design. Population is the whole authored corpus plus the showcase package MINUS the gate corpus, whose `FLAG[PD-n]` strings are proof fixtures rather than citations; that subtraction is a population fence, never an exemption table. AUTHORITY is `hard` because a citation lives in a COMMENT and the central engine refuses an ordinary finding whose token does not survive comment blanking, so the policy has no ordinary door to offer. A missing, empty or unreadable ledger member is a population-phase REFUSAL (the whole run is withheld), never a clean zero |
| `d-citation-integrity` | HARD/error, `analysis: resource`, `execution: entire-population`, family `text-citation`, `resources: [ledger:core-path-registry, authored-tree:docs, authored-text]`. CONVERTED (#1584). Every bare `D<n>` in the package sources AND in the core doc set resolves against the LIVE registry — it has a `^- **D<n>`-anchored ENTRY, or it falls inside the RESERVED RANGE the registry's own note declares. Both the entry set and the range are read off the registry's authored text, never a hardcoded ceiling: if the note is rephrased every reserved citation reds LOUDLY, which is the intended failure. Three fences, each with a proof row that dies without it: the `^- ` list-item pin (so the note's own bold D-numbers are PROSE, not minted entries — opening it silently resolves every citation of them), the non-word/non-hyphen left boundary (so `PD-n` and `<word>D<n>` substrings are not citations), and the RANGE-ANNOUNCEMENT fence — a `D<n>` followed by `+` is the registry announcing its next UNMINTED id ("next free number is D…+"), which is a statement about numbers that do not exist yet and must not dangle on its own bookkeeping. THE CORE-DOC ARM IS NEW IN THE CONVERSION AND WAS DEAD CODE BEFORE IT: the legacy descriptor filtered the ts-morph project, which globs `.ts`/`.tsx` only, so no Markdown member ever existed and the scope its own header claimed could not be reached. A missing or unreadable REGISTRY is a population-phase refusal (an absent identity INVERTS every judgment); a core doc the tree admits and the text door will not serve is a tool error, never a silent skip — except `empty`, which carries no citation to judge. AUTHORITY is `hard` because citations live in comments and doc prose, where the central ordinary engine refuses to bind |

ESCAPE: ONE adjacent `// @orb-waive config-group-completeness(<position>): <why + end condition>` — the position is the DECLARED NAME of the group or collection (`xCollection`), and on the host-import arm it is the keyword `import` that opens the offending declaration. The config twin of `modal-registry-completeness`/`section-registry-completeness`. **2026-09-06 (#1584):** the ANCHOR-OUTSIDE-REGISTRY arm is its own reviewed-grant policy, `config-anchor-in-registry` — the `state` / `features/config` path regex became two exact FILE-scoped grants in `lib/reviewed-grants.ts` |

The stamp test FAILS CLOSED: a call inside the name-prefiltered candidate set (the canonical export plus its import aliases) whose identity cannot be read at all is REPORTED, while one that provably binds something else — a same-named local function — is acquitted, which is `lib/origin-verdict.ts`'s classifier applied behind the prefilter that makes fail-closure safe. Known limit: stamps are deduped by file, so the grant identity is FILE-scoped by construction, and that dedupe is now pinned as a grant consumption count. **2026-09-12 (#2079):** the predicate reported only on a RESOLVED canonical export — an accusing arm whose test was an identity ACQUITTAL, fail-OPEN on unreadable, in a module whose `renderedModules` arm failed closed on the same question; the four grant verdicts (exact consumption, wrong operation, stale subject, aliased mint) are pinned in `tests/tooling/verify/gates/registry-family.test.ts`, which no test did before (split out of `config-group-completeness`, 2026-09-06, #1584) |

| `dangling-doc-cite` | HARD/error, `analysis: resource`, `execution: entire-population`, family `text-citation`, `resources: [tracked-files, authored-text]`. CONVERTED (#1584). #873 / Documentation-Law\.md §Relocation & retirement — the CODE side of `dangling-refs`: a source COMMENT naming a `docs/**.md` that the repository does not carry is RED, because a doc move that skips the citer sweep leaves a pointer to nowhere. TWO arms over one comment reader: **A** the ts-morph corpus, comments derived by diffing `blankTsComments`'s LENGTH-PRESERVING projection against the raw text (never a hand-rolled `//` regex — a `https://` inside a string literal ate the rest of its line in one); **B** the fileset the compiler corpus never loads, DERIVED and never listed — every repo-ROOT config with a comment syntax plus every shipped `packages/<pkg>/public` asset, which is where two of the four real lies lived. COMMENTS-INTENDED by design: scoping to comments is what keeps a proof-fixture STRING out of scope with NO exemption grammar, and the legacy allowlist is DELETED rather than re-homed (it was armed empty and no marker ever named this gate). ONE INVENTORY FOR BOTH QUESTIONS after the conversion — arm B's fileset and whether a cited doc exists both come from the git index, replacing a `readdirSync` walk and an `existsSync`; an untracked file is no longer a citer or a valid target, measured empty at conversion. The token grammar itself is the trailing-coordinate fence (`#` and `:` are outside its character class), which made the legacy trim and length guards dead code — both deleted. Vendor doc URLs are fenced by a lookbehind; an ELISION inside a path is prose. A derivation that resolves ZERO arm-B members is the blindness tripwire and is now a TOOL ERROR rather than a `line: 0` finding — "I could not judge" rather than "the tree is clean" — with no real-tree anchor guard, because a resource fixture owns its whole inventory. AUTHORITY is `hard` for two independent reasons: every finding sits in a comment, where the ordinary engine refuses to bind; and an ordinary owner of a whole-repository inventory demands every tracked waiver-format path as a carrier, which the authored reader refuses on the tree's committed symlinks |

DECLARED LIMIT: it proves the DECLARATION is coherent with the schema, never that a read applies the class's predicate — the membership rung is control-flow-dependent and the cross-tenant behavioral sweep stays that proof (AGENTS §1 "ownership is INHERITED, not stamped"; D18/D20/D23) |

Self-comparisons, unrelated owner operands, and comparisons that do not protect return prove nothing. The (a) table set is DERIVED per run from schema × `TABLE_SCOPING_ROWS`, with a blindness tripwire when empty. DECLARED LIMITS: READS only · no `T.id` predicate = LIST read · (b) membership tables out of scope (D20/D23; `packages/db/src/kit/fetch-owned.ts`) |

THREE shapes bite: the BY-ID write (`eq(T.id, …)`/`inArray(T.id, …)` with no owner predicate), the UNBOUNDED write (no `.where` at all — the shape the read half deliberately ignores as a list read, and which on a write is every owner's rows), and the UNRESOLVABLE TARGET (#769 — an identifier tracing to no declared table: a parameter, a reassigned binding, an alias chain past the depth cap or through a cycle; it reports under the `unresolvable-target "<ident>"` token instead of returning clean, because an unreadable target is an unproven one). The marker vocabulary is DISTINCT from the read half's on purpose — a promise about who may SEE a row can never silence who may DESTROY it, and each gate's stale arm stays independently two-sided. The (a) table set is DERIVED per run by `table-scoping-class`'s `ownerScopedTableIdents` (the shared readers live in `tooling/src/verify/lib/tenancy-read.ts`), with a blindness tripwire when the derivation comes back empty; the FULL table set (`schemaTableIdents`) is derived beside it as the denominator that separates a legitimately-out-of-scope non-(a) write from an unreadable one, and the shared alias resolver ignores the DECLARATION KIND (`const`/`let`/`var` name the same table). 29 markers at landing (the verb-chained owner loads · the D18 host rung, which is STRICTER than the stamp · the D20 un-principal engine planes: workloads runner/reaper, schedule tick, plugin activation, databank ingest, assets GC) plus ONE structural fix (`promoteActive`'s promote leg gained `eq(ownerId)` — the owner was already in its args). DECLARED LIMITS, each with a mustPass row: no post-fetch arm (a write's guard lives in the CALLER's control flow, unprovable structurally — that is what the marker records) · a write predicated on some OTHER column is a partition write, a different question · a CHAIN-FREE unresolvable statement stays out of the third arm (`cache.delete(key)` is byte-identical in AST to an unbounded `db.delete(T)`, and the pure-AST harness builds no type graph — the chained drizzle builders are the fence) · an `onConflictDoUpdate` upsert's collision is a conflict-TARGET question, not a WHERE — SUPERSEDED by the sibling `owner-scoped-upserts`, which owns that shape (the mustPass row stays as the two gates' seam) (D20/D23; `packages/db/src/kit/fetch-owned.ts`) |
| `owner-scoped-upserts` | the UPSERT half of the tenancy family: an `onConflictDoUpdate` on a `table-scoping-class` (a) table under `packages/server/src/**` is an UPDATE in disguise whose predicate is the conflict TARGET (a UNIQUE INDEX), not a `.where` — when that target omits the owner column, an insert carrying caller-supplied key values collides with a FOREIGN owner's row and the DO UPDATE arm overwrites it in place (the victim keeps its own `owner_id`, so every owner-filtered read afterwards shows the corruption as its own data). A second shape bites too: the UNRESOLVABLE TARGET (#769 — an insert identifier tracing to no declared table, reported under the `unresolvable-target "<ident>"` token; the `onConflictDoUpdate` in the chain already proves the statement is drizzle, so no further fence is needed here). Two arms clear it: the owner reaches the `target` / `targetWhere` / `setWhere` as the QUALIFIED `T.ownerId` (a per-owner unique index, or the one-line `setWhere: eq(T.ownerId, …)` belt when the index is legitimately global), or a two-sided `// @owner-scope-upsert-ok: <reason>` marker names WHO proved the target values are the caller's. `set` is deliberately NOT a guard property — assigning `ownerId` there restamps the row that already LOST the collision, upgrading an overwrite into a theft. FAILS CLOSED on a config the reader cannot see through (a `const CFG` identifier, a spread) and on drizzle's DEPRECATED ambiguous `where:`, which proves neither side. `onConflictDoNothing` is out of scope by construction. The marker vocabulary is DISTINCT from both siblings' — a promise about a `.where` can never silence a UNIQUE-index collision, and each gate's stale arm stays independently two-sided. The (a) table set is DERIVED per run by `table-scoping-class`'s `ownerScopedTableIdents` (shared readers in `tooling/src/verify/lib/tenancy-read.ts`, where `upsertConfigOf` is this half's answer to `whereArgOf`), with a blindness tripwire when the derivation comes back empty; the FULL table set (`schemaTableIdents`) is derived beside it as the unresolvable arm's denominator, and the shared alias resolver ignores the DECLARATION KIND (`const`/`let`/`var` name the same table). ZERO markers at landing: the full (a)-class census found exactly two ownerless-target upserts, both proven un-reachable cross-tenant by their caller chains and both given the structural `setWhere` belt anyway (`plugin_kv`'s FK-partitioned `(plugin_id, key)` PK → `eq(ownerId)`; the null-owner theme reseed on `themes.id` → `isNull(ownerId)`). DECLARED LIMITS, each with a mustPass row: `onConflictDoNothing` · (b) membership and (d) parent tables (D20/D23; `packages/db/src/kit/fetch-owned.ts`) |

Landed WITH its fix: `CopyCharacterBooks`/`CopyCharacterBooksOp` gained the owned-source `ownerId` gate (both ends re-checked in the op, red-first-proven cross-tenant carry). | `injected-op-caller-param-health` | family `injected-op-caller-param` (hard/error) — the two-sided whole-tree ratchet for the sibling's CALLER_FREE_OPS exemption table (a row naming an op that no longer exists, or that now takes a caller, is RED) plus the blindness tripwire on the shared `@orb/kit/ids` entity-id derivation; reuses the sibling's own AST attribution rather than a second reader for the same subject |
| `brand-in-name-position` | MECHANISM: a CENSUS JOIN, not a reference walk. One pass collects every type alias declared in `packages/kit/src/ids/index.ts` whose type carries the canonical `[brand]` property (`lib/id-brand.ts#canonicalIdBrand`, compiler-resolved — never a name pattern), lowercases its first letter into a POSITION NAME, and files a `population` receipt counting them. The same pass collects every Parameter / PropertySignature / PropertyDeclaration with an IDENTIFIER name and a bare-`string` type (`string`, or `string` unioned with only `null`/`undefined`). `evaluate` joins the two by exact name. So the vocabulary is DERIVED: a brand polices its own position the day it is minted, and there is no path list to rot. BLINDNESS: an empty derivation is no longer a bespoke check — the central `receiptFailures` `count === 0` predicate turns the zero-member receipt into a policy REFUSAL, which every proof row is implicitly pinned by (re-probed 2026-09-12 by neutering the collector: all rows fail with `population "kit-id-brand-positions" resolved zero members`). ESCAPE: the central ordinary-waiver plane — `// @orb-waive brand-in-name-position(<position>): <reason + end condition>`. **The legacy `@foreign-id-ok` grammar is RETIRED and nothing parses it**; a marker in the old spelling suppresses nothing (census 2026-09-12: 76 live `@orb-waive` markers, ZERO live `@foreign-id-ok` — its two remaining occurrences in the repo are the module's own retirement sentence and a negative fixture). Stale, dead-position, malformed and over-broad markers are now the CENTRAL plane's `AUTHORITY ALARM`s, not findings of this policy. POPULATION: the seven authored PRODUCT roots (`@authored` minus `@tooling`/`@scripts`); `@tests` is deliberately IN, because a fixture is exactly where a raw id gets hand-minted into a typed call. DECLARED LIMITS, each with a proof row: EXACT lowerCamel only (`ownerId`/`fromChatId` would need the hand-kept skip list this policy exists to avoid) · a local VARIABLE is out of scope (the defect is the SIGNATURE) · a position typed through a NAMED alias is invisible to this syntactic reader · a TYPE ALIAS is not a signature position · a canonical brand alias declared outside the ids home mints no position · a QUOTED name (`{ "chatId": string }`) is not claimed, held by TWO mutually redundant clauses (0 live sites against 116 unquoted, measured 2026-09-12) |
| `detached-work-traced` | fire-and-forget work whose FAILURE IS INVISIBLE — one class that recurred SIX times under `packages/server/src/**`. ARM A1: an ExpressionStatement whose promise chain ends in a DISCARDING rejection handler (an empty/constant-returning arrow — the error is thrown away and nothing at all happens) and which calls NO span opener. Detached work outlives the request that dispatched it, so by the time it runs the request root has SEALED: a parented span is dropped as a late orphan and the failure reaches no log, no trace and no caller. ARM A2: inside a span opener's callback, a `catch` that does not RETHROW, or a DISCARDING `.catch(…)` nested in that callback — `withRequestSpan` seals `status:"ok"` unless the callback rejects, so a warn-and-return catch paints the trace dashboard green while the work fails. The absorbing `.catch(() => undefined)` on the OUTSIDE of the opener call is the CORRECT shape and passes. THE SPAN VOCABULARY IS DERIVED: an opener is an exported function of the tracing module that calls OTel `startActiveSpan` with `root: true`, directly or through another derived boundary — the family keys on the MECHANISM (the detach is what makes the ring seal the bucket), so a rename keeps it honest. CONVERTED to `defineGate` (#1584) and SPLIT: the occurrence arms are this `ordinary`/`error` policy, the blindness tripwire is the `hard` sibling `detached-work-traced-health` under the identical family, because §12.1 allows one authority per descriptor and a tripwire on the detector's own liveness must never be waivable. `analysis: "types"`, population `@server`, `execution: "entire-population"` (the vocabulary is derived from another file), and both halves read the shared reader `tooling/src/verify/lib/detached-work.ts`. PROMISE LINKS ARE READ IN BOTH SPELLINGS (2026-08-27, #751): `p.catch` and `p["catch"]` are the same property read, over literal / parenthesized / literal-typed / `+`-concatenated / same-file-`const` keys — a dot-only reader went silently green on `void p['catch'](() => undefined)`; a DYNAMIC key stays a declared limit rather than a guess. ESCAPE: the private two-sided `@swallowed-ok(<position>)` grammar is RETIRED for the central `@orb-waive detached-work-traced(<position>)`; its stale, malformed and over-broad arms are the central engine's, once, for every policy. The POSITION is the guarded WORK's own authored text — the whole callee chain (`entry.promise`, `a.cancel`) or its member name where the chain carries a paren or newline (`where`), and for a swallowing catch the caught BINDING or the keyword `catch` — computed through the same anchor contract `caught-failure-ownership` uses, so a site governed by both reports one position under both. The legacy synthetic `_2` disambiguator is GONE and unreplaceable (it appeared in no source); two same-named dispatches on one line are separated by the callee chain plus the central engine's carrier containment, which is MEASURED by a proof row rather than asserted. Five live markers were translated in the conversion commit (legacy 5 = current 5, no drop, no split). The `@swallowed-ok` SPELLING survives with exactly one remaining owner, `pnpm ast`'s swallowed lens, whose grammar requires a colon immediately after the tag and whose subject is exported declarations under `packages/` — disjoint from this family in both form and scope, so the translation could not change its verdict. DECLARED LIMITS, each with a mustPass row: only DISCARDING handlers count (a handler that logs is already visible) · only STATEMENT position (a nested per-item `.catch` in a `Promise.all` map is not the class) · a bare `void work()` with no handler is out (an unhandled rejection is loud) · an absorbed promise ASSIGNED to a variable is out (someone still holds it) · "traced" is satisfied by ANY opener call in the statement · A2 reads only a syntactic `throw` (a rethrow via a helper is invisible) · and the family CANNOT prove a span is MEANINGFUL — that its name, id or attributes correlate to the work it wraps |
| `detached-work-traced-health` | the BLINDNESS TRIPWIRE half of the `detached-work-traced` family (#1584 split): ZERO root-span openers derived from the tracing module means the occurrence policy is scanning for a vocabulary that no longer exists and has gone silently green, so the empty derivation is itself the finding. `hard`/`error` — and hard for two independent reasons: a waiver here would silence the detector's own liveness, and the finding is FILE-anchored on the derivation SOURCE, which has no authored token an ordinary waiver could bind to. `analysis: "syntax"`, population `@server`, `execution: "entire-population"` — which natively replaces the legacy arm's hand-rolled real-tree anchor probe and scope test (a narrowed request now DEFERS the policy instead of letting it fire on a subset), both deleted rather than ported. The tracing module's ABSENCE is a TOOL ERROR, not a clean pass: a run whose derivation source does not exist is one this policy cannot render a verdict for. An INVENTED row pins the mechanism rather than the call: `root: false` still calls `startActiveSpan`, so a reader keyed on the opener NAME reads that tree as healthy and only the `root: true` test discriminates |
| `caught-failure-ownership` | issue #751 — **a syntactically handled rejection is not ownership.** Reports UNPROVEN OWNERSHIP (never "bug") over `packages/*/src` + `tooling/src`, **both `.ts` and `.tsx`**, in three arms: `promise:<work>` — a `.catch(h)`/`.then(_, h)` whose handler DISCARDS the failure, in every spelling (dot or bracket key — literal, parenthesized, literal-typed, `+`-concatenated, same-file `const` — optional access, and `call` / `apply` / `Reflect.apply` / a bound alias, because a method-syntax-only reader leaves each of those as a silent laundering route); `empty:<binding>` — a CatchClause whose block AND its `finally` name no owner; `default:<binding>` — a catch whose only escape is a constant fallback (`null`/`[]`/`{}`/`-1`), which changes behaviour while leaving the failure contract unstated. **Every owner is PROVENANCE-checked, because the failure mode is syntax cosplay:** the governed pino/`getLog` logger with a contextual message (not a local `{ warn() {} }`), the governed `notify`/toast manager carrying the failure with non-success copy (not a locally-declared `notify`), a genuine React `useState` SETTER slot — tuple element `[1]`, not a value merely NAMED `setError` — fed a failure-VALUED argument, TanStack query/form and `createEntityMutation` with a failure-valued `errorToast`, `withRequestSpan`, a rethrow, a NATIVE `Promise.reject(err)` (a local object named `Promise` does not launder), a named handler that itself owns, and an error-as-data outcome a real consumer reads. An explicit SUCCESS discriminator REFUSES ownership: `{ status: 'ok', error: err }` carries the error as incidental data beside a success verdict. Provenance dies on REASSIGNMENT — a governed initializer overwritten by `x = fake` (including `&&=` and a `for..of` write, and including hoisted writes that textually precede the declaration) owns nothing after. CONVERTED to `defineGate` (#1584, 2026-09-11): one `ordinary`/`error` policy on the shared reader `verify/lib/caught-failure.ts`, population `["@packages", "@showcase", "@tooling"]`. ESCAPE: ONE adjacent `// @orb-waive caught-failure-ownership(<position>): <owner/surface or why safe + end condition>`, reconciled by the CENTRAL ordinary-waiver engine (malformed / unknown / stale / dead-position / over-broad / duplicate) — the shared vocabulary, no private allowlist and no baseline. **The position is the EXACT SOURCE SLICE the policy reports at, never a label:** the caught binding (`err`) or the keyword `catch` for a bindingless clause; the guarded WORK's callee text for a promise absorber (`save`, `a.save`, `p`), falling back to its member name for a multi-line chain. The retired private vocabulary was `promise:<work>` / `empty:<binding>` / `default:<binding>`; 574 live markers were re-derived from the real run and translated in the conversion commit. The legacy arm that accepted a LIVE `@swallowed-ok` owned by `detached-work-traced` is RETIRED (§12.5 bans a gate-specific exemption grammar); those five sites now carry their own `@orb-waive` beside the untouched `@swallowed-ok`. DECLARED LIMITS, each with a mustPass row: a DYNAMICALLY keyed rejection link is unreadable, not assumed · zod's `.catch()` combinator is schema construction, not a promise · package build/config files outside `packages/<name>/src` are out of the governed corpus · `tests/` and `scripts/` are outside scanRoot (the pinned harness boundary — tests are golden/cleanup behaviour) · an owner or rethrow routed through an opaque helper is invisible to a syntactic reader |

Shares the one-homed D-cite rows in `lib/ledger-banned-shapes.ts` with `schema-banned-shapes`, which is a shared VOCABULARY and not a shared family — the two policies read different subjects, so this one is a singleton family under its own id (2026-09-06, #1584) |
| `warning-code-coverage` | the emit-coverage RATCHET for both structured warning-code tuples (`WARNING_CODES` in infra/providers · the domain warning set) — every member must reach an executable warning record/emit call bound to the canonical warning collection/emitter or its proven alias; arbitrary literals and warning-shaped pushes to unrelated receivers do not count, and a canonical tuple that stops resolving fails loud (D41/D45/D48/D51). A vocabulary that stops resolving takes its receipt to zero members and WITHHOLDS the policy — the rename tripwire owned by the runtime, not by a finding (all three arms plus a positive control that the withholding is the rebinding and not the fixture: `tests/tooling/verify/gates/registry-family.test.ts`). FAMILY SINGLETON WITH ITS REASON: `tupleVocabularyFact` is a shared PRIMITIVE that three unrelated families read, not a family key, and no sibling policy judges whether a warning code is EMITTED.
