The gate is a thin whole-project consumer of the shared static-class provenance substrate: Oxide tokenizes only semantically proven values, runtime `dark:` prefixes are a separate AST arm, unresolved provenance fails loud, and cross-file producer/consumer changes defer together. `dark` is banned as a VARIANT, never as a utility: the candidate is split on TOP-LEVEL colons only — colons inside an arbitrary selector or function are data, so `[&_.x:dark:y]:bg-card` passes — and the search skips the final segment, so a class ending in `dark` names a utility and passes too. Its proof rows cover alias/member/re-export composers, local/cross-file values, arrays/objects/templates, false-positive prose/selectors, and scoped deferral, with `execution: "entire-population"` deferral pinned separately because a narrowed request must not declare a producer dead |
| `tokens-contract` | token-contract-program.md §4 (#936) — the base token vault, exact Light/Mocha value-set membership, bounded Hearth/Light/Mocha Resolver, removed portable-path + exact CSS-target ledgers, and hash-pinned DTCG 2025.10 Format/Resolver schemas are one fail-closed contract. Official schemas own portable shapes; closed `orb.*` extensions own output roles, explicit `theme\|root` placement, guidance, and concrete runtime CSS values. Its scanned-entry census is the base/Light/Mocha constituent sum pinned by `tests/tooling/token-contract.test.ts` and printed on the gate's own `check:structure` scan line, never a count copied into this row — in the receipt's SOURCE label since #2292, because the receipt's `members` must be what the policy MEASURED (the seven bundle documents) and a token census is 0 for exactly the unparseable vault this gate exists to report, which withheld the whole policy instead. CONVERTED (#2182 — `a97454714`'s own subject line; this row cited #2183, its NEIGHBOUR's issue, until #2294): a final `hard` resource policy on the closed `token-contract` bundle, singleton family, `validateTokenContractTexts` preserved verbatim. THE REMOVAL RATCHET SURVIVED, and how is the load-bearing part — a policy has no root and cannot shell git, so the merge-base vault is read by the PROVIDER and published as `removalBaseline`, the same shape `tracked-files` uses for `git ls-files`. Its three statuses are distinct BY DESIGN: `ready` compares, `unavailable` emits a `removed.baseline` diagnostic, and `empty` (a repository with no commits — every conformance fixture) is SILENT, because a history that never existed is vacuous rather than blind and the alternative puts a finding on every consumer's clean corpus. The ratchet is unreachable from a proof row for that same reason and is pinned by a `runPolicyPass` against the real root with the vault overlaid to drop a token |

| `no-vanity-alias` | one symbol, one name — a workspace rename-import (original not otherwise present in-module), a rename-export of a UNIQUELY-homed symbol (the ChatSource class — a name with <2 producer modules), or 2+ bare type-aliases onto one identifier (RouteOverlay/RoutableChat). Sanctioned: a genuine in-module collision; a GENERIC name (≥2 producer modules — barrel disambiguation); an `@orb/ui` / db-`*Table` / contracts-`*Wire` rename; a `packages/server/src/**/contract/**` distinct-alias-per-verb vocab home; any vendor-package rename. A `defineGate` ordinary policy, `execution: "entire-population"` over the seven `packages/<pkg>/src/` roots (rule b counts producer modules tree-wide); the reported position is always the ORIGINAL (unaliased) name |
| `no-raw-intl-time` | raw Intl API or `.toLocale*()` usage is forbidden. Production reads time from the injected clock (`@orb/kit/time`), never ambient now. The kit time seam's own construction is an exact reviewed grant. (Spine-TypeScript-and-Patterns.md) |
| `no-await-db-in-loop` | an AWAITED Drizzle query inside a loop is an N+1 — one round trip per iteration (Spine-TypeScript-and-Patterns.md §8). Query identity is the called METHOD'S OWN DECLARATION inside the installed drizzle-orm package through the shared client-call reader, so `deps.db.select()`, an aliased handle, `db["insert"]()` and the relational `db.query.x.findMany()` are all the same query while a same-named method on a local class is not. The loop test is a BOUNDED ancestor walk up from the delivered await that stops at the first function boundary, so an await inside a callback the loop merely CONSTRUCTS is not executed per iteration. ONE ROUND TRIP PER ITERATION IS ONE FINDING: the report dedupes by waiver carrier, because two findings sharing a carrier and a token make every marker over-broad. THE #944 THIRD ANSWER: a receiver the checker cannot bind, called with a method spelled like one of the ten query verbs, is REPORTED under a disjoint message pinned by a `messageIncludes` row (#2041) — an untyped seam is where a real handle hides. DECLARED LIMITS, each with its row: a non-verb method on an unbindable receiver is out of subject, and the test tree is excluded by both its root and its basename |
| `persistence-no-in-memory-state` | an ambient `Map`/`Set`/`WeakMap`/`WeakSet` constructed in a `persistence/` file — persistence is queries-only; in-memory state (caches, registries) belongs in a named subsystem, not the query layer (Core-0-Architecture-and-Structure.md §7). The subject is the AMBIENT GLOBAL constructor through the shared global-origin reader, so `globalThis.Map` and an immutable alias are the same constructor while a local `class Map` and an imported implementation are different ones. THE #944 THIRD ANSWER (repaired #2041): the fail-closed arm asked only whether the constructor bound ANY declaration, which no fixture could reach — the proof harness loads the default lib files, so a bare `Map` always binds the ambient one and always took the ordinary arm — and which ACQUITTED the one shape it existed for, since an import door naming `Map` and resolving to nothing still binds an ImportSpecifier. The arm now asks the shared module-member reader and classifies its refusal: a resolvable import and a local class both pass, an unreachable door is REPORTED with a disjoint message pinned by a `messageIncludes` row. The row that advertised the old arm was replaced rather than kept, because it was green through the ordinary verdict. DECLARED LIMITS: the candidate gate is the SPELLED name, so an aliased construction is not offered and a same-shaped ambient global outside the four keyed collections is out of subject; the member-path depth test is UNFALSIFIABLE and says so in the header |
| `no-loose-id-cast` | MECHANISM: a syntax visitor over `AsExpression` with TWO arms and no origin reader. Arm 1, `as never` — pure syntax, and it disables every assignability check. Both fences are proof-pinned since #2047: a SINGLE cast into a brand passes (tsc still checks assignability, so only the `as unknown` double erases everything), and a double cast to a NON-brand target passes (this is an id-brand policy, not a general cast ban). THE REPORTED POSITION IS THE CAST OPERAND, and it differs between the arms — `value` for `as never`, the whole inner `value as unknown` slice for the double — so both are now named by `token` expectations and an author waives the operand, never the `as` clause. NO #944 THIRD ANSWER EXISTS, recorded with the construction attempted (#2041): an unreadable target type hands back an empty property list indistinguishable from every legitimate non-brand target, so a fail-closed arm would accuse the whole population; that identity is held by tsc, one tier up the ladder. For a branded-ID parameter use `castId`/`brandedId`/`parseId` from @orb/kit/ids; for a genuine ORM/library escape, `@orb-waive no-loose-id-cast(<operand>): <reason>` (Spine-TypeScript-and-Patterns.md §4) |
| `no-mint-via-cast` | MECHANISM: a HARD ban composed of two shared-reader identity questions and nothing else. Is this call the canonical kit `castId` (`lib/id-brand.ts#createKitIdCallMatcher`, the same matcher `no-fake-disabled-id` uses)? And does its FIRST ARGUMENT's callee resolve to a library mint — `MODULE_GENERATORS`, a generator VOCABULARY of five module/export pairs, or the ambient `crypto.randomUUID` global? `castId` re-brands a value that already IS an id; minting goes through `mintTypeId(ID_PREFIX.x)` or `newId<T>()`. Every fence is proof-pinned since #2047, including both halves of the GLOBAL arm — a different member of the same global (`crypto.getRandomValues`) and a DIFFERENT trusted global declaring the same member (a `@types` lookalike, because the ambient resolver trusts only TypeScript's lib files and `@types` packages) — plus the export-vs-member fence and the vocabulary table itself. THE #944 THIRD ANSWER (repaired #2041): both the seam matcher and the generator reader used to answer an unresolved origin with "not a subject", so a subject the checker could not read was ACQUITTED by the one policy whose job is to forbid the shape. Both refusals now route through the shared three-answer classifier — a callee that PROVABLY binds a non-module declaration (a string method, a request accessor, an injected op) still passes, a door with no reachable target is REPORTED with a disjoint message pinned by `messageIncludes`. Measured before the flip: 20 call-argument and 221 cast-seam candidates live, zero unreadable, so the repair admitted no new finding (Spine-TypeScript-and-Patterns.md) |
| `no-hover-display-swap` | the HOVER-OSCILLATOR class, made structural (packages/client/src/components/row-reveal.ts) — a class token whose variant chain carries a hover key (`hover:`/`group-hover[/name]:`/`peer-hover[/name]:`, their `not-` negations, and the arbitrary `[&…:hover]:` selector form) and whose terminal utility SETS DISPLAY (`hidden`/`block`/`flex`/`grid`/`inline*`/the `table-*` display family/`contents`/`flow-root`/`list-item`) anywhere in `packages/{client,ui}/src` is RED. A hover-keyed display swap removes a box from layout under a STATIONARY pointer: the line/row reflows, the hover boundary slides across the cursor, the box returns, and the surface flips at frame rate — MEASURED on the preset list (2026-08-02, real mouse): ~1,727 pointerover/out pairs, ~85 crossings/sec, with ZERO DOM mutations (pure CSS, no React, and invisible to every CT — a synthetic pointer does not re-hit-test on a layout shift, which is why this must be static). Fix = reserve the box, swap the PAINT (`invisible`, or `opacity-0`→`opacity-100` per `ROW_REVEAL`/`ROW_REVEAL_SWAP`). A DEVICE-class display swap (`pointer-coarse:hidden`, `pointer-fine:flex`, a breakpoint) is legal by construction — it cannot change while the pointer moves — and `[@media(hover:hover)]` is a capability query, not a pointer state. DELIBERATELY UNFENCED (no className/`cn()` carrier scope, unlike `no-off-token-radius-shadow`): the flagged shape needs a hover VARIANT prefix, which no English sentence carries, and the carrier fence measurably MISSED a live offender (a class string assigned to a variable before reaching its slot fn). **The ALLOWLIST ratchet is RETIRED** — it drained to `{}` at the landing commit and the table went with the conversion; this cell said "currently EMPTY" for a day after there was nothing to be empty (caught 2026-09-12 by the lane converting its sibling). DECLARED blind spots: a runtime-ASSEMBLED class string, and hand-authored CSS (`sanctioned-css-homes` path-closes the product tier) |

WHY ARM B IS TABLE-NAME-MATCHED rather than every `*Row`: `*Row` is also the repo's word for a read-time PROJECTION (`LeaderboardRow`, `MomentumRow`, `MsgRow`, `DigestRow`, `ReuseRow`, … eleven legitimate hand-written aggregates) — a blanket rule would have shipped eleven false reds; the match narrowed it to three, of which ONE was a real defect (`WorkloadScheduleRow`, derived in the landing commit) and two are homonyms with cited ALLOWLIST rows (discovery `ThemeRow` = a theme CLUSTER, not the `themes` palette table; stats `ModelStatRow` = a GROUP BY projection with computed percentiles). Both-ways allowlist ratchet. Rot the gate cannot see: a re-spell under a DIFFERENT name — that is the `pnpm ast respell <domain>` candidate lens's job, deliberately never a gate |
| `contract-derives-not-respells-health` | family `contract-derives-not-respells` (hard/error) — the RENAME/RESHAPE tripwire for the sibling's ARM-B ALLOWLIST: a `<file>::<ShapeName>` row that no longer matches a live shape/table pair is a permission nobody uses and is RED, unsuppressibly, because the promise it audits is exactly the door the sibling's ordinary marker would reopen. | `two-class-role-authority` | an inline `role === "host"` comparison in an ENFORCEMENT position (the comparison, through `!`/`&&`/`\|\|`/parens, is the condition of an `if` whose branch THROWS) anywhere under `packages/server/src/domain/` is RED unless the file is a CITED chokepoint. Spine invariant #6: the privilege comparison lives at the domain's ONE authority chokepoint, so a surface that relaxes or tightens its gate cannot drift from every other surface — and the chokepoint, not the verb, owns the leak-free not-found-vs-forbidden shape. THE TWO CLASSES (ruled 2026-08-03): (1) ENFORCEMENT — decides whether an operation may proceed → `domain/chat/substrate/auth/decide.ts` (`assertHost`/`permitsHost`, routing to the injected `can()`) and `can()` itself at `domain/admin/guard.ts`; (2) DATA PROJECTION — produces a payload/view FIELD consumers thread as data → `substrate/member-visibility.ts::viewerReadsHidden` + `substrate/chat-detail.ts::viewerIsHost` (D106-F1, deliberately Principal-free), which the gate does NOT bite: the enforcement-position test IS the line between the classes. CONVERTED (#1584) to a `reviewed-grant` policy that reads its literal set from `PARTICIPANT_ROLES`' own declaration and proves the AXIS by the read's TYPE — which retires the transport-tier blind spot below BY CONSTRUCTION, since a StreamAuthority tier is not the participant vocabulary. Three exact rows in the central reviewed-grant table (`tooling/src/verify/lib/reviewed-grants.ts`): `domain/admin/guard.ts` (the `can()` seam), the chat NOMINEE target-validity check, and `chat/persistence/participant.ts`'s character-seat shape invariant (invisible to the legacy hardcoded `host` literal because it names `member`). Landing commit collapsed SIX rpg verbs onto `guard.ts::assertHostRole(role, reason)` (byte-identical refusals, pinned by tests/server/domain/rpg/authority.suite.int.test.ts) and routed chat's fork gate through `permitsHost`. STAGE R1 (2026-08-03) then retired the transitional second home: `RpgContext` gained the injected `can`, `guard.ts`'s `assertHostRole`/`assertOwnUserRef` became a catch-and-reword over the kernel (chat's `permits()` pattern — the refusal sentences are still rpg's, the comparison is not), and the `domain/rpg/guard.ts` row was deleted in the SAME commit the reroute landed. The row was built two-sided precisely so that commit would self-red without the deletion — it did, and that red is the migration receipt; the authority suite passed UNMODIFIED across the reroute. DECLARED blind spots: `entry/compose/automation-plugin.ts` (an authority PAYLOAD, class 2, outside `domain/`), `transport/…/sources/automation.ts` (compares a StreamAuthority TIER, a different axis sharing the lexeme), an aliased compare (`const isHost = …` used in a later `if`), and the guard-INVERSION spelling (`if (role === "host") return; throw …` — the throw is outside the `if`); each has a mustPass row so the limit is written, not assumed. The three rows are held TWO-SIDED (drifted operation, renamed subject — each licenses nothing and alarms STALE), beside the vocabulary-withholding arm that already refused an absent or relocated `PARTICIPANT_ROLES` |

| `duplicate-action-doors` | issue #252 — the §13 more-than-one-home IA class made structural ("new chat lives in three places"). One tRPC MUTATION invoked from N distinct components inside ONE rail section is the same verb wearing N doors on a plane a user sees whole. A final `defineGate` policy (`action-doors` family, `authority: "reviewed-grant"`, `analysis: "syntax"`, `execution: "entire-population"`, population `@client` under `features/**` + `state/**`). NOTHING is hardcoded: the procedure key IS the `trpc.<…>` property path read through the shared door grammar (`_shared/trpc-doors.ts`, the same resolver the `ast subset-callers` lens uses), and the plane is DERIVED — `SECTION_IDS` resolved from its one home through `lib/tuple-read.ts` (#947) crossed with the co-located `features/<owner>/lib/*-section.tsx` definitions, recognised by carrying BOTH a vocabulary `id` and a `rail` field. A feature owning no rail section is still ONE plane, keyed on its own directory. THE RULED UNIT IS THE DOOR SET, NOT A CARDINALITY (#2101), AND THE CONVERSION KEEPS IT: the grant `subject` is the pair key `<plane>::<procedure>` and the `operation` is `duplicate-action-door-set:<every live door, path-sorted>`, so a third door, a moved door, a SWAPPED door or a pure RENAME changes the string, the ruling stops matching, the finding is effective and the orphaned row alarms `stale-reviewed-grant`. ITS EXEMPTION MECHANISM IS THE CENTRAL REVIEWED-GRANT TABLE: the retired `duplicate-action-doors.baseline.json` (six RATIFIED count rows) and the retired gate-local `EXEMPT_PROCEDURES` (one procedure row, four qualifying planes) are TEN exact rulings authored in `tooling/src/_shared/action-door-rulings.ts` and mapped into `lib/reviewed-grants.ts` — `_shared` because the lens must read the same rulings and a lens→`verify/index.ts` import closes a measured cycle. One finding per pair (§5's aggregation rule), through `lib/reviewed-grant-findings.ts`, anchored at the first door in path order with every door named `file:line`. TWO GUARANTEES CHANGED AND BOTH ARE STATED RATHER THAN ABSORBED: (1) #2101's `new-doors` arm anchored each unruled door AT ITS OWN FILE and the aggregated finding does not — a policy may not read the grant table, and the retired arm was measurably wrong about it anyway (the planted third `chat.forkChat` door made the count arm accuse `message-actions-row.tsx`, the door that was there first); (2) a NEW settings-section door, or a pure rename of any sanctioned door, now REDS until its ruling is re-pointed, where `EXEMPT_PROCEDURES` absorbed the first case silently. The two §4.6 BLINDNESS TRIPWIRES moved to the `hard` sibling `duplicate-action-doors-health`. DECLARED BLIND SPOTS, both stated in #252 and both owned by the runtime half (design-audit's same-role-and-name lens): a registry-rendered action is ONE call site behind N rendered slots — which is exactly how the founding "new chat" complaint escapes this tier — and a responsive pair is N call sites behind ONE rendered door. Further declared limits (each a `mustPass` row): two doors on two DIFFERENT planes is not the defect; the unit is the COMPONENT, so one file wiring a verb twice is one door; only the `trpc.` proxy names a procedure. Proofs run on the conformance stage; the conversion differential, the population controls, the central grant identity in three directions and real-corpus liveness are in `tests/tooling/verify/gates/action-doors-family.test.ts` |

Both of the family's derivations are NAME-KEYED, so either one coming back empty would silently regroup every door under its feature directory while the census still reported a number — and a reviewed grant could license that away, which is precisely what a tripwire must not permit. TWO arms, mutually exclusive and ordered: VOCABULARY — `SECTION_IDS` resolved to ZERO members, so no definition can be recognised as a rail section at all; SECTIONS — the vocabulary resolved but ZERO definitions matched it, so the plane map is empty. The vocabulary arm returns first, because with no vocabulary the plane map is empty by construction and reporting both would be one defect wearing two findings. An absence verdict anchors through the shared subject-anchor rule (`lib/absent-subject-anchor.ts`) rather than on its own missing subject, which a policy cannot report on. THE LEGACY REAL-TREE ANCHOR HAS NO SUCCESSOR AND THAT IS THE CONVERSION WORKING: the descriptor gated both arms on `packages/db/src/schema/index.ts` purely so its OWN conformance mini-projects would not trip them — a fixture fence, not a predicate — and final proofs run on isolated projects, so each arm is now driven by its own fixture. | `class-token-splice` | issue #249, FINAL `defineGate` policy (ORDINARY/error, `analysis: types`, `execution: entire-population`, population `@client`+`@ui` — 1,687 authored sources, byte-identical to the legacy `scanRoot`). THE UNIT IS THE JUNCTION, not the interpolation: a template literal reaching a class carrier is flattened into segments (head literal, each span's resolved VALUE SET, each tail literal) and every seam between two adjacent segments is judged. A seam is SAFE when the LEFT's every possible value ends in whitespace OR the RIGHT's every possible value starts with it; an EMPTY possible value is TRANSPARENT, so the seam is re-judged against the segment beyond it — which is why the live `shrink-0${cellWidthClass(c)}` idiom (value set `" w-avatar-hero" \| ""`) stays green. An expression that resolves to NO literal set is UNSAFE: Tailwind's scanner reads WHOLE LITERALS out of source text, so a composed token registers no rule and paints NOTHING, silently past tsc, biome and eslint's `better-tailwindcss` (which skips substituted templates entirely — measured 2026-08-19), and the permissive direction is the one that ships dead paint. Values resolve through ternaries, `\|\|`/`??`, names and single-return `function` declarations to depth 4 — identity through the SHARED binding readers (`resolveStableExpression` / `resolveCallableOrigin`, with the `lexicalReferenceSymbol` fallback for a module-local factory), so an alias, a re-export rename and a cross-file const all resolve, and a REASSIGNED binding refuses and lands on the unsafe arm. A callee's returns are read from a `ReturnStatement` VISITOR INDEX keyed by the enclosing function, never a descendant walk — and because that index is built from the files a run walks, the verdict does not compose over a subset, which is what `entire-population` declares (the policy defers under `--changed`/`--scope` and is decided by the whole `pnpm check`). The finding is reported ONCE per template, anchored on the INTERPOLATED EXPRESSION at the offending junction, so the waiver is `@orb-waive class-token-splice(<that expression>)`; where that text carries a paren or a newline (any call) the marker grammar cannot hold it and the runtime derives the first paren-free token instead. The runtime half of this class is `no-unknown-classes`, which validates static tokens against the engine's real utility set. Comment-SAFE by construction (it subscribes to `TemplateExpression` and reads only that node's own literal spans). DECLARED LIMITS (each a mustPass row): only `className`/`class`, a `cn`/`cx`/`clsx`/`cva`/`tv`/`twMerge`/`twJoin` argument, and a `base`/`class`/`className` config key are judged — a template anywhere else is prose; a resolution chain deeper than 4 hops reads as unresolvable; and the EMPTY-transparency rule is permissive where a non-empty left value composes (`p-2 ${flag ? "" : "flex"}x` passes), carried verbatim from the legacy rule and pinned so a change to it is a decision rather than a drift. Zero live findings over the real tree on BOTH dispatchers, proven against a planted control |

The central table's liveness subsumes both legacy staleness modes: a factory that moved and a factory that stopped calling `persist` are the same zero-consumption STALE row. The KEY vocabulary is the subject by design (an untyped options bag has no contextual type to key on) and is read through the shared property-name reader, so a computed key is the same knob. `temperature`/`maxOutputTokens`/`topP` name a sampling posture and nothing else; `maxTokens` is SHARED VOCABULARY with the canon-window budget, so it is a subject only when its own literal also carries an unambiguous knob. The VALUE is read through the shared numeric reader, so a signed literal and a value one binding hop away are the same buried constant — UNLESS it derives from a CATALOG HOME (the preset catalog or the pure side-gen-posture ladder, each with its own row), which is the sanctioned rung. DECLARED LIMITS, each with its row: a lone `maxTokens` is a transcript budget, a value the ladder RESOLVED is a member read of a call and is refused, `infra/**` is the wire/usage-accounting layer and is outside the population, and specs are excluded by basename |
| `no-manual-token-estimate` | hand-rolled `.length / 4` token estimates — use `@orb/kit/tokens` instead |
| `no-media-queries-in-features` | viewport breakpoint variants in features — use `@orb/ui` responsive primitives |
| `no-multiplexed-mutation-error` | multiplexed mutation errors (`a.error ?? b.error`) — use a single mutation |
| `no-pointer-variants-in-features` | FINAL ordinary/error occurrence policy for pointer/hover capability variants in feature string carriers outside app-shell. Shared `ui-tier-permissions` evidence retains the feature predicate and explicit capability-token grammar; hover state, pointer-events utilities and container queries remain accepted. Exact central waivers license deliberate occurrences. |
| `pointer-capability-tier-permission` | FINAL reviewed-grant/error owner for the live app-shell capability home. One candidate aggregates zero or many capability occurrences; the exact central grant preserves the prospective shell permission without removing its source from the census. |
| `pointer-capability-tier-health` | FINAL hard/error owner for an absent feature-root census or missing app-shell home. A missing feature root reports once rather than also accusing its nested shell. Findings anchor inside the client policy population; shared family tests retain the legacy blindness and home controls. |
| `no-raw-container-widths` | UI-Architecture-and-Layout.md §4 — a hardcoded content width anywhere it can reach the DOM as a class. The banned shapes are `w-<digits>` / `max-w-<digits>` / `(max-\|min-)w-[<length>]`; `w-1/2`, `w-full` and `min-w-<digits>` floors stay legal. THE SCAN IS DELIBERATELY UNFENCED (#1954): every string literal in the population is split on whitespace and each CLASS FRAGMENT is matched, with NO `className`/`cn()` ancestry test — in this repo most class strings live in `tv()` variant maps, `cva`-style records and exported constants, and a carrier fence would blind the gate to exactly that surface. The reported position is the offending FRAGMENT, not the enclosing literal, which is what makes the ordinary waiver `@orb-waive no-raw-container-widths(<fragment>)` spellable. Population is `@client` + `@ui` MINUS `packages/ui/src/layout/**` and `packages/ui/src/markdown/**` — the two primitives that IMPLEMENT the container-width scale. The remedy is `<Container size="sm\|md\|lg">`, never "layout tokens" |
| `no-raw-matchmedia` | a raw `matchMedia` read — the AMBIENT GLOBAL, judged by the RECEIVER so a structural cast of `globalThis` cannot dodge it — outside the named media-query one-homes. Every exception is a reviewed-grant row keyed to the reader's own file, not a class of file: each of the render-time and imperative reduced-motion homes, the shell's viewport hook, the imperative coarse-pointer home, and the media grid's own pointer-capability read holds its own exact `(subject, operation)` row in `lib/reviewed-grants.ts` with a `why` and an `endsWhen` — a home that moves reds at its row instead of carrying an exemption into the void. The media grid's row is the one-time translation of a permanent inline marker (the marker itself is deleted — a reviewed-grant policy has no inline door); its `why` names the mismatch it still needs a grant for (its query is `(pointer: fine)`, which the coarse-pointer home does not answer), never a missing home once one lands. DECLARED NARROWING: a bare `typeof` capability probe is not a read; DECLARED LIMIT: a reflective `Reflect.get` access names the api in a string argument, so there is no member node to judge. The limit recorded here until 2026-09-11 — that `window`/`self`/bare spellings land on the fail-closed unreadable finding because the analysis program is DOM-less — was FALSE and is WITHDRAWN (#1584 exemplar audit): the live pass and the conformance runtime both build ts-morph projects with DEFAULT compiler options, hence the default lib WITH DOM, so every root spelling resolves and carries the PRECISE message. The fail-closed unreadable finding is reached by an opaque-receiver read (its own `mustFlag` row); what a DOM-less program would do is pinned in `tests/tooling/verify/gates/home-client-family.test.ts` |
| `no-context-provider` | React 19 deprecates `<Context.Provider>` — render `<Context>` directly instead (Spine-TypeScript-and-Patterns.md §1) |
| `no-forward-ref` | React 19 deprecates `forwardRef` — pass `ref` as a normal prop instead (Spine-TypeScript-and-Patterns.md §1) |
| `no-use-context` | React 19 deprecates `useContext` — read the context with `use(Context)` instead, which also works in a conditional position (Spine-TypeScript-and-Patterns.md §1). TWO ARMS, KEYED BY ORIGIN RATHER THAN BY NAME: the import DOOR React's `useContext` enters through, and any CALL whose callee RESOLVES to that export. The legacy name-keyed mechanism (comparing callee text against `useContext` / `React.useContext`) is DELETED — it passed an import alias and a namespace member silently and red a local helper of the same name. A reference whose origin the shared readers cannot place is REPORTED, never passed (#944). Ordinary authority over `@authored`, so a deliberate site takes `@orb-waive no-use-context(useContext)` — the position is the export name at the reported node; the row's old "No exemptions" line described the retired shape |
| `no-manual-memo` | Final reviewed-grant React-origin policy for useMemo/useCallback/memo. The installed compiler end-condition is enforced by no-manual-memo-compiler-health. |
| `no-manual-memo-compiler-health` | Hard React-origin resource policy: the installed React Compiler bundle must still contain its virtualizer denylist signal while the manual memo permissions depend on it. Removing that signal requires retiring the affected grants and memo calls; unreadable installed resources refuse. |
| `no-legacy-react-api` | Final ordinary React-origin policy for retired React/React DOM APIs and non-boundary class components. Canonical bindings cover aliases and namespace members; actual error boundaries remain legal. The library compatibility occurrence carries its central positional waiver. The legacy marker parser and stale-marker accumulator are deleted. |
| `gate-ignore-inventory` | Temporary hard migration-residue check for real legacy `@orb-gate-ignore` comments across authored and showcase source. These comments cannot suppress final policies; the check prevents inert text from looking like an active exception. Strings and prose quotations remain inert. It does not implement waivers: `@orb-waive` reconciliation owns ordinary exceptions. Reassess retirement with the legacy runtime at #2176; the old registered/consumed/malformed suppression distinctions are retired. |
| `no-manual-autosave-flush` | a single `features/**` function body calling BOTH a structural array op (push/remove/insert/moveFieldValues) AND `handleSubmit` — the retired §7-trap call-site flush; the D78 session-boundary factory's store driver autosaves structural array ops (autosave-form-doctrine.md §7 G-A; armed at the SEAL 2026-07-16) |
| `assets-single-writer` | only `domain/assets` writes the `assets` table + calls `storeBlob` — the one CAS+row coherence site (D21 context); an importer of `storeBlob` or a raw `.insert/.update/.delete(assets)` outside the sanctioned home is RED Final reviewed-grant/error policy: all candidate writer modules are judged before exact central grants license the sanctioned writers; stale grants remain errors. |
| `audit-client-tests` | five structural test anti-patterns grep cannot see, over every `tests/**/*.test.ts(x)` (Spine-Testing.md §5): a test callback with no matcher-chained `expect`, an `async` test with no `await`, a bare `expect(x);`, a `describe()` with no nested test, and an empty lifecycle hook. CONVERTED to `defineGate` (#1584): `ordinary`/`error`, `analysis: "types"`, SINGLETON family, population `@tests` filtered by FILENAME to `*.test.ts`/`*.test.tsx` — the filename IS the population, not a shortcut for one: widening to all of `tests/**` would admit the `.test-d.ts` type suites, which assert with `expectTypeOf` (not a matcher chain) and would have been accused wholesale. `.ct.tsx` cannot match `*.test.tsx`, so the CT lane's exclusion survives by construction. Reconciliation is per-FILE node-range containment over a per-file index built on the one shared walk — file B's `expect` cannot satisfy file A's stub, pinned by a fixture whose numeric offsets deliberately overlap. Each arm carries its OWN message and its OWN authored position — the test callee, the callback's `async` keyword, `expect`, `describe`, the hook's name — so two arms firing on one test call stay separately waivable; the legacy synthetic labels (`no-assertion`, `async-no-await`) were not authored text and gave the ordinary authority no working door. DECLARED LIMIT: assertion-helper resolution is SAME-FILE only (an imported helper's symbol declares an `ImportSpecifier`, which has no body), true of the legacy gate too and now pinned by a row. OVERLAP, measured and deliberately NOT merged: `test-no-stubs` owns the same no-assertion verdict at `hard` authority but resolves no helpers and recognizes only `test`/`it`/`.skip`, so a `test.each(…)(…)` stub is invisible there and caught here |

A final `defineGate` policy (family `bus-payload`, `reviewed-grant`, `analysis: "types"`, `execution: "entire-population"`, population `@contracts`) over the shared `busPayloadFact` provider (`tooling/src/verify/lib/bus-payload-fact.ts`), which performs ONE transitive walk of every live wire-event union's own type IDENTITY (#948): `extends` bases, intersection constituents and aliased union arms are followed through imports, so a credential inherited from an imported carrier is reported at its declaring site; a NAMED type a field REFERENCES is deliberately NOT resolved (a referenced payload like `MessageView` is separately homed) while what a field spells INLINE is read. A merged (same-named, twice-declared) carrier is walked in EVERY declaration, not just the first (#1030 F2). The house §5.5 mapped-type distribution `{ [K in <union>]: <template> }[<union>]` is RESOLVED rather than waved through (#1047): the index erases the mapped keys, so the wire fields are the TEMPLATE's members and the constraint is resolved into its finite member set (an inline literal union, an alias chain, or `(typeof TUPLE)[number]` read through `tuple-read.ts`) before the template is walked once. FINDING GRANULARITY IS GRANT GRANULARITY: a reviewed grant matching more than one candidate licenses NOTHING, so the policy reports ONE finding per distinct smelling field NAME through `lib/reviewed-grant-findings.ts`, anchored at the first declaring site with every site's line in the message. Every way the reader can FAIL to establish a wire shape — an unresolvable base or schema, an open key space, a hollowed-out root, a root name that resolves to nothing — belongs to the `bus-payload-allowlist-health` sibling, which shares the family and the provider and differs only on authority |
| `bus-payload-allowlist-health` | the UNSUPPRESSIBLE half of the bus-payload firewall (D16), split out at the #1584 conversion because it differs on AUTHORITY: `hard`, identical `family` `bus-payload`, same `busPayloadFact` provider, same `@contracts` population. It reports every way the shared wire-shape reader can fail to ESTABLISH a bus member's shape, and there is no door because the thing a door would license is unknown by construction. AN OPEN KEY SPACE is refused rather than skipped (#1024): an index signature — which `getProperties()` omits outright — a `Record<…>`, an `unknown`/`any` field, or a mapped type whose key space is not a finite string-literal union declares no key vocabulary in any home, so nothing anywhere could scan what rides inside it; only `ChatBusEvent` has a type-level pin for that invariant, so for `UserBusEvent`/`DomainEvent`/`WiBusEvent`/`notificationEventSchema` this policy is its sole enforcer. BOTH READER POSITIONS FAIL CLOSED (#1066): the identity walker and the FIELD walker agree, so a conditional field type and an inline object inside a TUPLE element are refused rather than riding the wire in silence — the field walker has four terminal answers and no fifth: WALK the wrappers/containers, READ an inline literal or a provable distribution, STOP at a NAMED reference, or REFUSE `unsupported-shape:<Kind>`; only a provably KEYLESS kind passes in silence, a set deliberately WIDER than the identity walker's. `.loose()`/`.passthrough()`/`.catchall()` are the zod spelling of the same refusal. TWO TRIPWIRES with deliberately DIFFERENT guards: a root that still resolves but yields ZERO wire members — no field, no deferral to another named root, no revisit, no loud refusal — reds its own empty-denominator arm anchored on that root's declaration (#1030 F3, and it needs no corpus guard because it judges only roots this run RESOLVED), while the §4.6 BLINDNESS sweep reds a root NAME that resolves to nothing and abstains below a corpus COUNT rather than a file list, because an all-homes-loaded guard would abstain exactly when a home moved. The blindness verdict is an ABSENCE and anchors through `lib/absent-subject-anchor.ts`; the shape LABELS ride the message rather than the position, because this contract requires a position to be authored text and `unresolved-base:<Name>` appears in no source file |

| `ct-no-oneshot-live-read-assert` | a HARD invariant (Spine-Testing.md §7, the DEF-14 flake class) — a component test (`*.ct.tsx`) never reads mutable async state with a non-retrying `expect()`, including when a captured local is consumed through property/element access or a value transform; use a web-first assertion or genuinely re-read inside `expect.poll`. A settled atomic snapshot may use exactly one adjacent `// ONESHOT-OK: <concrete reason>` marker; bare, malformed, overbroad, and stale markers RED |

The FIELD name and the `type` TELL are read through the shared property-name reader (so a computed `["schema"]` key and a computed `type` key are the same members, and a key with no static value is refused), the `"object"` value through the shared static-string reader (so a const-held tell still counts), and the VALUE through the shared binding resolver — which follows a module-local const AND AN IMPORTED CONSTANT to the literal it names, a deliberate widening over the legacy same-file-only arm. DECLARED LIMITS, each with its row: a member-MUTATED builder root is an assembly rather than an authored literal and is not followed, the tell is `type: "object"` so a non-object JSON-Schema literal is out of subject, a projected call is not an object literal at any number of binding hops, and specs are excluded by basename |

| `scrubber-home` | security (§3.6 member-strip; the `ed2aafc5` cold-scrubber leak) — the stateful hidden-span stream scrubber (`@orb/kit/content::createHiddenSpanStreamScrubber`) is constructed ONLY in its producer home `domain/chat/substrate/member-visibility.ts`: per-subscription scrub state cannot survive replay→live handoffs (a cold scrubber mid-`<lie>` forwards the secret's tail, and the withheld-open stall is a reconnect ORACLE); an import or call anywhere else in `packages/*/src` is RED. Cited standing exemption inside the home: `scrubStreamReplayForMember`'s unwired token-log cold-start (needs the producer-stamp treatment IF ever wired). The single row is held TWO-SIDED (drifted operation, renamed subject — each licenses nothing and alarms STALE), which matters more here than anywhere else in the family: this is the security half of the boundary and its grant is the only thing standing between a reviewed producer stamp and an unreviewed one |
| `scrubber-factory-home` | the DEFINITION half of the §3.6 member-strip boundary, split off `scrubber-home` at #1584 because the two arms differ on AUTHORITY. `scrubber-home` resolves every reference against the factory's declaration home, so a `packages/kit/src/content/` that stops EXPORTING `createHiddenSpanStreamScrubber` silently retires that trust boundary rather than breaking it: every reference resolves elsewhere, the policy finds nothing, and a rule about stateful scrub state renders a clean pass over a subject it no longer has. HARD — a completeness claim about a definition site is neither a permission nor a per-occurrence mistake, so there is nothing to grant and nothing to waive. Two distinct failures, both loud: a content directory holding no source takes the population receipt to zero and REFUSES the run; the directory existing while no module exports the factory is a finding |

Layout constraints, intrinsic/fraction values, `min-h-0` and unsized layout/media APIs stay outside the predicate. Computed className, namespace tags and CSS-variable shorthand remain declared limits. Two content-width Select occurrences carry exact ordinary markers; central authority detects stale or wrongly positioned permissions. Declared proofs preserve the legacy predicates; `ui-size-via-variant.test.ts` owns alias/limit, population, isolation, product-marker and stale-marker controls. |

Axes used to live in CLASS STRINGS only, so two authored arms of one primitive in one home were indistinguishable and the ui-audit walker's authored-target identity folded them into ONE decision — one repair row where two decisions exist, with a muddled min–max range. A final `defineGate` policy (`ui-variant-axes-stamped` family over `lib/variant-axis-stamp.ts`, `hard`, `entire-population`, `@ui`), THREE arms: A1 an unstamped stamped-axis recipe · A2 a `tv()` config the reader cannot resolve, FAIL-CLOSED (unknowable axes = unknowable compliance) · A3 two recipes sharing an exported NAME, which is the consumption key. Each carries its own message discriminator so a proof row can pin WHICH arm fired. THE TRANSITION RATCHET IS GONE: `ui-variant-axes-stamped.baseline.json` held 11 rows at mint (tranche 1 = the seam + 4 pilots: Button, Badge, Toggle, Skeleton), was DRAINED to `{}` by #1097 (tranche 2, 2026-09-02 — all 15 stamped-axis recipes now reach the seam), and the ledger, its generator, the `baseline` verb row and the `debt` row were deleted with the conversion; ZERO rows were carried, so the arm that policed stale rows dies with it. DECLARED LIMITS: `packages/ui/src` only (no `tv()` recipe exists outside it — swept); only a `tv()` initializer is a recipe; the axis vocabulary is CLOSED to four names Base UI never emits, so a variant axis can never shadow a runtime state attribute. | `ui-variant-axes-stamped-health` | the §4.6 AXIS-VOCABULARY TRIPWIRE for `ui-variant-axes-stamped`, its own HARD `entire-population` policy in the same family whose population is EXACTLY `packages/ui/src/lib/variant-attrs.ts`: the home must still yield a readable `STAMPED_VARIANT_AXES` tuple AND still declare the `variantProps` function, because the recipe policy DERIVES its whole subject from both. Each missing half is its own finding at the home (two reds when both are gone, never one and never silence); an ABSENT home is a population-phase REFUSAL, which replaced the legacy `packages/ui/src/lib/class-merge.ts` real-tree anchor. Its fences ACQUIT, so every one of its falsifiers is a `mustFlag` going green rather than a `mustPass` going red |
| `suppressions` | EVERY foreign-tool lint/type suppression directive in authored TS/TSX is licensed by an exact REVIEWED GRANT naming its RULE CLASS and its SCOPE, or it is a blocking finding with NO door. Detection is the shared directive reader (`suppressionSites` over `readDirectiveComment`, the same grammar `no-blanket-suppression` judges for the opposite reason): exact comment directives only — `biome-ignore` plus `-all`/`-start`/`-end`, every `eslint-disable` variant plus `eslint-enable`, and `@ts-expect-error`/`@ts-ignore`/`@ts-nocheck` — matched at the comment OPENER, so prose and string mentions are inert. Three carriers, deduped per line: leading ranges, TRAILING ranges, and comment-only JSX expression containers. The population is the authored roots plus the showcase package, minus the captured foreign SillyTavern runtime; the GOVERNED SCOPE (`source` or `tests`) is the grant OPERATION, because a test suppression is permanent for a different reason than a source one (#962) and a ruling in one scope is not a ruling in the other. Finding granularity EQUALS grant granularity — ONE finding per `(rule, scope)`, anchored at the class's first site with every site named in the message — because central reconciliation licenses a grant that matches EXACTLY ONE finding and calls two or more OVER-BROAD. Liveness is the engine's: a ruled class with zero live sites is consumed zero times and alarms STALE; a class with no row is effective and BLOCKS. **AUTHORITY MIGRATION 2026-09-12 (#2063), and the trade is measured, not inherited:** this replaced a committed both-ways per-file COUNT RATCHET plus two gate-owned ratification tables (`suppressions.baseline.json`, 281 file rows / 587 occurrences; `RATIFIED_RULES` 46 rows, `RATIFIED_TEST_RULES` 7). **The ruled half got WEAKER** — per-occurrence cardinality is gone, so a new marker of an already-granted class in a new file is invisible where the ratchet REDded it; the measurement that bought that is a ruled half growing 545 → 560 occurrences in one week while the burnable half moved by ZERO. **The unruled half got STRICTLY STRONGER** — no door at all, red on sight, where the ratchet budgeted it as admitted debt. The EXCEED, STALE and CLASS-DRIFT arms died with the counts; the STALE-RULE arm survives with a stronger successor owned by the engine, which also catches the over-broad direction the legacy arm had no word for. Measured at conversion through the real reader over the real project: 597 occurrences · 283 files · 65 `(rule, scope)` classes · 65 grant rows, every one consumed exactly once, zero effective findings, and zero grants stale on arrival |

IT DID NOT SPLIT: the census's recorded arity of 2 (sanctioned doorway · warning debt) was a DISPOSITION axis, not a predicate axis — the five DEFERRED rows scattered across arms A/B/B2 and the one DOORWAY row sat in arm F-write, so splitting on it would have double-reported every unwired knob (standing law §2). Arm A: every `EffectiveAppConfig` field has a TYPE-keyed behavior consumer in `packages/server/src` — fields come from the interface's RESOLVED type, so a field INHERITED through `extends` is a subject and an `extends` clause binding no interface declaration REFUSES (#1094). Arm B: every `USER_SETTINGS_SECTIONS` member has a `section:"x"` write path (client patch or compose seed) — members resolve through `lib/tuple-read.ts`, so a member arriving via a local/imported SPREAD counts and reports at the CONTRIBUTING tuple's element. Arm B2: every `appSettingsSchema` key has a write field in the admin surfaces (`features/settings` ∪ `user-admin`). Arm C: every local settings `z.object` leaf plus every top-level leaf reached through the precise imported semantic-source manifest has a read-shaped occurrence in `{server,client,ui}/src` (name-keyed, documented lenience). Arm F: every `chatMetadataSchema` key has BOTH a write verb (`domain/chat/verbs` ∪ `transport/trpc`) and a read outside the parser + write scope. Arms B2/C/E/F share ONE authored-object member reader: object spreads, `{ ...base.shape }` spreads, `.extend`/`.merge` composition, local/imported schema bindings, shorthand members and computed string-literal keys RESOLVE; every other member kind, a key-changing or unmodelled builder method, an unbound binding, a composition cycle and a zero-member spread REFUSE (exit 2) — a knob population this reader cannot establish never reads as a smaller one. EVERY FINDING ANCHORS ON THE MEMBER'S OWN AUTHORED NAME NODE, where the legacy descriptor reported every arm at `<contract>:1:0`. AUTHORITY IS `reviewed-grant` BY CONSTRUCTION: arm F's write and read belts report the SAME node, and arms B2 and C both report an `appSettingsSchema` key's name node, so §6.2 forbids an ordinary door promising independently waivable findings there — the identity is `(member subject, arm operation)`. THE TWO RETIRED `ExemptionTable`s (`DOORWAY` 1 row · `DEFERRED` 5 rows) ARE SIX CENTRAL ROWS in `lib/reviewed-grants.ts`, and their hand-rolled STALE and ORPHAN arms are both the central `stale-reviewed-grant` alarm — a grant consumed zero times after a complete owner run is an error whether the member GAINED its wire or VANISHED. Nullability is DERIVED from the shared Drizzle fact (`drizzleSchemaFact`) every run — zero hardcoded column lists, so a widened column arms the gate in the same commit. For a fact the AST cannot see (an inner join on that same column already excluding NULLs), the one central positioned waiver `// @orb-waive nullable-column-inequality(Table.column): <why + end condition>`; stale, malformed and over-broad markers are central authority findings. | `baseui-surface-manifest` | THE BASE UI VERSION-BUMP TRIPWIRE. A final `defineGate` policy (`baseui-read` family, `hard`/`error`, `analysis: "resource"`, `execution: "entire-population"`, no source population at all). The installed `@base-ui/react` public surface must equal the committed ledger, and every anatomy part in it must carry an adjudicated disposition (`exposed` \| `sealed-away`+reason \| `n-a`); the birth state `unresolved` is always RED, so a bump cannot be absorbed without a human ruling. BOTH SUBJECTS ARE DECLARED RESOURCES — the ledger through `json:baseui-manifest`, the installed package through `installed-package:base-ui` in BOTH its `ast` mode (the declaration paths the surface is derived from) and its `metadata` mode (the version) — so the legacy `readdirSync`/`existsSync`/`readFileSync` reads are gone and the two hand-rolled blindness FINDINGS they needed are now RUNTIME REFUSALS: an absent or unparseable ledger withholds the owner at the POPULATION phase, an uninstalled package is an `[evaluate]` tool error, and either way the run is exit 2 rather than a green zero. The surface is derived — not listed — from each component module's own `index.d.ts`/`index.parts.d.ts` export specifiers, and each part's IDENTITY tuple is `kind\|symbol\|from\|props\|state\|inherits`: the ALIAS TARGET, the fully-expanded prop names, the `<Part>State` keys and the heritage arms, so a re-typing that keeps the exported name is a visible diff. `state` joined that tuple at the conversion, closing the hole where a bump could move the very `data-*` surface `baseui-state-data-attributes` judges seals against while this tripwire said nothing. FOUNDING RECEIPT: the 1.6→1.7 structural diff showed `Select`/`Combobox`/`Autocomplete` `Separator` re-implemented per component and `Drawer.Handle` re-pointed off dialog's `DialogHandle` — both filed upstream as "fixes", both material changes to types our seals consume; only a surface diff catches that class. SCOPE is deliberately the PUBLIC surface (internal churn would otherwise red every patch release). Carries the reader's OWN blindness tripwire — a part the expander learned nothing about is RED, and so is one truncated at the depth ceiling, minted after a depth cap of 8 silently reported `Combobox.Root` as 14 props when it has 44 — plus the SINGLE-OWNER arm for a ledger that is valid JSON and is not a ledger, which every other policy in the family stays silent about because a file-anchored ordinary finding has no waiver position. TWO ARMS: PARITY (a reader key absent from an ENUMERABLE writer key-set — `caption_meta` verbatim) and UNPROVABLE (the column has named readers but its writers carry an open-typed value end to end, so the vocabulary is undecidable — the defect CONDITION, fixed by CLOSING THE TYPE; fires once per column at its first reader, only when exactly one open column owns the reads). The table mirrors the mixed loader's discovered roster — every `status:"active"` legacy descriptor plus every
canonical `defineGate` policy (336 registered gates); the discovered roster (`loadMixedGateCorpus`,
`tooling/src/verify/lib/loader.ts`) is the runtime truth.

**MERGE-BOMB HAZARD IN THIS TABLE: escape every `|` inside a cell as `\|`.** A bare `||` (a JS operator in a
description) widens the table's column count, and `pnpm format:docs` then rewrites **every row** — a 280-line
reflow that lands as a conflict against every sibling lane editing this file. The tell is a `format:docs` diff
far larger than your edit. Measured 2026-09-12.

A row's description is normally an INDEPENDENT summary. A row that instead COPIES its gate's runtime
`message` is a coupled site — the copied string is the one a violating agent actually reads — and it
must DECLARE itself by ending the description cell with `(@mirrors-message)`; `enforcement-registry-parity`
then holds the pair byte-for-byte, REDs a declared mirror that drifted, and REDs an undeclared copy that
is byte-equal to the message (a copy that does not say it is a copy is the row that rots unnoticed —
`no-if-is-group` taught retired vocabulary for months). A PARAPHRASE is prose and is not judged: mirror
fully and declare it, or summarize.

The owner-scoped write/upsert family resolves named `@orb/db` import aliases through the canonical import
specifier before judging target identity. `characters as characterRows` therefore carries the same tenancy
contract as the unaliased spelling; a cosmetic rename cannot evade either half.

The 7th fired-trigger gate (PD-116), `solo-byte-identical`, is NOT a static gate — it is the
cross-cutting property suite `tests/server/domain/chat/solo-byte-identical.suite.int.test.ts`: two
identically-shaped one-character chats (untouched-solo config vs fully group-configured) drive ONE
round each through the REAL engine and the wire request + persisted canon must be BYTE-identical
(D16 "solo is a group of one"; the behavioral half of the `no-if-is-group` gate).

### Layer 3 — DORMANT structural gates (built + self-tested, deliberately `status:"dormant"`)

These gate files exist in `tooling/src/verify/gates/` and each carries its own `tests/tooling/` self-test
proving it fires, but their descriptor's `status` field is `"dormant"` by decision — `report.ts`'s
`loadGates()`/`runPass` filters to active descriptors only (each finds real debt whose backfill rides
a later wave, or gates a construct that doesn't exist yet — keeping them off preserves green-to-commit
without hiding the debt). Activation is flipping the descriptor's `status` field to `"active"`. The
`DORMANT_GATES` set in `tests/tooling/check-gates.repo.int.test.ts` is a mirror of this, not ground truth —
the descriptor's `status` field is ground truth.

| Gate | Enforces | Activation trigger |
| - | - | - |

None currently — `component-size-ui` and `audit-client-tests` (formerly listed here) are `status:"active"`
now (see the Layer-3 ACTIVE table above), and `monotonic-tests` was DELETED WHOLE in #2217 (owner ruling)
along with its committed test-baseline manifest.

## Layer 4 — dependency-cruiser (`.dependency-cruiser.cjs`) — **ACTIVE**

The import-graph backstop ("boundaries are physics"), wired into `pnpm check` + pre-push. The config owns
the rule inventory and severities: the package cake (kit←contracts←db←server←client, + `@orb/ui` between contracts and client), server tier direction
(entry→transport→domain→infra→foundation→kit), kit-purity + kit/ui no-node-builtins, infra-no-db, foundation-reaches-up-to-nothing,
drivers-through-domain, domain isolation (no-cross-feature/-verb/-subsystem + front-door + substrate
mediation), the client rules (feature front-door, no-cross-feature, no-backend-runtime, `confirm-uses-composite` — client-architecture-lockdown.md §16 G7, `features/**` may not import `@orb/ui/alert-dialog` (the raw primitive) — ConfirmDialog is the sole feature-tier confirm, `client-components-tier` — client-architecture-lockdown.md §16 G5, the tier-2 `components/` seal (never imports `features/`/`routes/`/`main.tsx`; `lib/` and `state/` never import `components/`), `client-composition-tier-door-only` — client-architecture-lockdown.md §3/§7, the composition-tier dir modules (`client/src/agent-nav/`, `client/src/agent-seed/`) are imported by `main.tsx` alone (the `client-nothing-imports-main` mirror — otherwise a feature reaches a foreign front door THROUGH them and every hop passes), `client-compose-door-only` — client-architecture-lockdown.md §5/§7, the door's assembly half (`client/src/compose/`) is imported by `main.tsx`, `routes/router.tsx` (the #43 lazy `/` boundary) and a `compose/` sibling ONLY — same backdoor, and this module imports every feature front door, the `@orb/ui`
satellite seals — D42/D52 physics), providers public-surface + strategy-isolation + vllm-surface-isolation + the transitive credential firewall
(openrouter ↛ agent-sdk), persistence-no-io, stats-no-vector-tables, `not-to-dev-dep` (prod `packages/*/src` must not import a
pure devDependency; `recommended-strict` omits it), and `no-orphans` (severity `warn` — a module
nothing imports; `knip` is the deep authority, this is a cheap graph-level smell). Every rule is **pinned by
`tests/tooling/dependency-cruiser.int.test.ts`** (derives the rule set from the config, fires each on a
fixture — anti-drift). The full feature set (err-long, mermaid graph, `--focus`/`--reaches`/`--affected`)

- deliberate non-adoptions are documented in the config header.

The direct client/backend type connection is confined by `client-backend-types-only-through-trpc` to
`packages/client/src/data/trpc.ts`, and by `client-trpc-type-target` to the server's root `AppRouter` type
export. `client-no-backend-runtime` continues to reject runtime backend imports. This is an intentional
inferred API type dependency; it does not remove the router's transitive compiler closure.

`pnpm knip` / `pnpm knip:prod` (`knip.ts` config, all issue types as errors) is the
dead-code/dead-export/dead-dependency lane — LIVE in `pnpm check` as the `deps:knip` static-tier stage.

## Layer 5 — jscpd (`jscpd.json`) — copy-paste detection

Structural duplication the per-file biome/gate rules can't see. Scans `packages/**` (TS + TSX + CSS);
the centralized `tests/` mirror is outside that native path selection, while migrations, fixtures, and
`*.d.ts` are explicitly excluded. **CI lane, not the pre-commit fast check** (whole-tree scan). Gate: the build fails
over **2%** duplication (`threshold`) — ratchet down as the codebase matures. `pnpm cpd` (console) /
`pnpm cpd:report` (HTML → `reports/cpd`). Active now: vacuous on the comment-only placeholder tree,
fires the moment real code lands.

## Layer 6 — Stryker (`stryker.config.js` + `stryker.gate.config.js`) — mutation testing — **SCHEDULED (Phase 4c/5)**

Mutates source + reruns the suite to score whether tests actually *catch* bugs — the "test covers the
line but asserts nothing" signal coverage can't detect (neo's founding `isVllmBackend`-lying-gate
class). Two lanes: `pnpm test:mutation` (exploratory, `break:null`) and `pnpm test:mutation:gate`
(pinned to the highest-stakes pure modules — chat routing/assembly, credential resolution — fails the
build below `thresholds.break`). On-demand + CI, never in `pnpm check` (runs are minutes). Configs +
targets exist; `break` stays null (both configs) until a measured score calibrates it
(`core/Spine-Testing.md` › Mutation testing).
